We report a 22-year-old man with autism spectrum disorder and a remote history of laparotomy for ingestion of batteries and magnets who presented for recurrence of absence seizures. A radiopaque intragastric foreign-body conglomerate was identified incidentally on radiography. Plain abdominal imaging demonstrated numerous linear, serpiginous radiopaque structures conforming to the gastric contour without evidence of bowel obstruction or perforation. Upper endoscopy showed the gastric cavity to be almost completely occupied by a dense network of intertwined cables, with associated erythematous and erosive gastropathy. Endoscopic retrieval with a metallic snare was unsuccessful because of the volume and entanglement of the material. The patient subsequently underwent open supraumbilical laparotomy and anterior gastrotomy, allowing en bloc removal of a bezoar composed predominantly of mobile phone charging cables. The gastrotomy was closed in two layers, and a closed-suction drain was placed. Recovery was uncomplicated; oral intake was progressively advanced, the drain was removed at discharge, and follow-up at one and two weeks showed adequate wound healing and no gastrointestinal symptoms. Psychiatry established a diagnosis of pica and arranged outpatient follow-up. This case highlights the potential for clinically silent, prolonged accumulation of unusual foreign material in the stomach and the importance of integrating radiographic, endoscopic, surgical, and psychiatric management when endoscopic extraction is not feasible.
Neuromodulation Therapies for Depression: Current Status and Future Directions.推荐
PMID 42985368 · Alpha psychiatry · 2026 10 10
作者：Matsuda Y, Sakuma K, Igarashi S, Hayashi D et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：Major depressive disorder is a leading cause of disability worldwide, and many patients fail to achieve sustained remission with first-line pharmacotherapy or psychotherapy. This unmet need has traditionally been framed as treatment-resistant depression (TRD), defined by an inadequate response to antidepressant treatment. Within this context, neuromodulation has assumed an increasingly important role by providing mechanistically distinct interventions when medication-based approaches are insufficient, poorly tolerated, or too slow. In this expert opinion article, we present a targeted, clinically oriented review of two established neuromodulation therapies for depression, electroconvulsive therapy (ECT) and transcranial magnetic stimulation (TMS), encompassing conventional repetitive TMS (rTMS), deep TMS, and theta-burst stimulation (TBS), as well as magnetic seizure therapy (MST), the most clinically advanced investigational convulsive modality. ECT remains the benchmark treatment for severe, urgent, psychotic, or otherwise refractory depression due to its rapid and robust efficacy, although its use is limited by anesthesia requirements, cognitive adverse effects, and associated stigma. TMS has the strongest evidence base among nonconvulsive neuromodulation treatments, with pivotal sham-controlled multicenter trials supporting conventional rTMS and deep TMS, while TBS protocols are improving feasibility, access, and scalability. MST is conceptually appealing as a more focal convulsive treatment designed to preserve antidepressant efficacy while reducing cognitive burden; recent Confirmatory Efficacy and Safety Trial of Magnetic Seizure Therapy for Depression (CREST-MST) data have strengthened its evidence base, but its routine clinical role remains to be defined through regulatory approval, durability data, and implementation evidence. We argue that future progress will depend not only on the development of new stimulation protocols but also on improved clinical sequencing, patient selection, and maintenance strategies that help sustain acute gains. The concept of difficult-to-treat depression (DTD) broadens the clinical framework beyond antidepressant nonresponse to include persistent burden, partial response, tolerability constraints, functional impairment, and patient-centered goals. This perspective may inform earlier and more strategic use of neuromodulation, although most randomized controlled trials remain focused on TRD. Further studies are needed to determine whether DTD-based approaches improve real-world outcomes. Advances are likely to arise from integrating protocol refinement, individualized targeting, scalable home-based approaches, depression-relevant extensions such as bipolar depression, and durable maintenance strategies rather than identifying a single universally superior modality.
The First Open Fetal Surgery for Myelomeningocele Repair in a Bicornuate Uterus With Anterior Placenta in Central America: A Case Report From Nicaragua.浏览
PMID 42985099 · Cureus · 2026 10 04
作者：Avilés Castillo AC, Suárez García ME, Rodríguez JL, Gonzalez R et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：病例报告
摘要：Open fetal surgery for myelomeningocele (MMC) has shown superior outcomes compared with postnatal repair, but it remains unavailable in most low- and middle-income countries (LMICs). We report the first open fetal MMC repair performed in Central America, in a patient with a bicornuate uterus, anterior placenta, and a gestational age beyond conventional criteria. A 27-year-old woman (BMI: 28.6 kg/m²) with epilepsy treated with carbamazepine and late prenatal care was referred at 26 5/7 weeks for a fetus with lumbosacral MMC, lemon sign, bilateral ventriculomegaly (10.5 mm), and an obliterated posterior fossa. A bicornuate uterus with an anterior placenta was identified. After multidisciplinary evaluation involving Maternal-Fetal Medicine, Fetal Surgery, Pediatric Neurosurgery, Neonatology, and Anesthesiology, open fetal microneurosurgery was performed at 27 5/7 weeks using microsurgical myeloplasty with continuous intraoperative Doppler monitoring. Postoperative surveillance showed stable ventriculomegaly, regression of posterior fossa herniation, and an intact repair site. Cesarean delivery at 33 weeks was performed for severe oligohydramnios secondary to premature rupture of membranes, resulting in the birth of a 2,060 g male with Apgar scores of 8/9 and intact lumbar closure. The neonate required eight days in the neonatal intensive care unit (NICU) and did not require cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) diversion. Follow-up to postnatal day 47 confirmed a favorable neurological outcome. Open fetal MMC repair was feasible in an LMIC hospital with appropriate multidisciplinary coordination, even in the presence of a bicornuate uterus with anterior placenta and a gestational age beyond conventional criteria. During the documented follow-up period, the neonate showed favorable short-term neurological outcomes without the need for CSF diversion.
Implementing the mhGAP-HIG: the process and evaluation of training primary healthcare workers in Khyber Pakhtunkhwa, Pakistan.浏览
PMID 42985004 · BJPsych international · 2025 09 10
作者：Humayun A, Najmussaqib A
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：BACKGROUND: To address the treatment gap for common mental disorders in low- and middle-income countries facing humanitarian challenges, it is crucial to build the capacity of primary healthcare workers (PHCWs) and integrate mental healthcare into primary care settings. AIMS: To investigate the effectiveness of a Mental Health Gap Action Programme Humanitarian Intervention Guide (mhGAP-HIG) adapted for use in Pakistan to build the capacity of PHCWs in Khyber Pakhtunkhwa. METHOD: Six mhGAP-HIG training workshops were conducted, each lasting for 5 days, across six districts of Khyber Pakhtunkhwa. A total of 105 PHCWs (74 primary care physicians and 31 clinical psychologists) were trained through these workshops. We used multiple triangulations for data collection and analyses. Paired-sample t-tests were applied to compare scores on knowledge questionnaires pre- and post-training and after 8 months. We also conducted thematic analysis to examine participants' feedback regarding the training, and performed content analysis on the participants' reflections on the adapted guide. RESULTS: Our findings demonstrated significant improvements in PHCWs' knowledge related to the mental health conditions in the mhGAP-HIG. Their scores improved by 12.08%, increasing from 73.86% pre-training to 85.94% post-training. Noticeable improvements in knowledge were recorded for the modules 'Harmful use of alcohol and drugs' (22.56%), 'General principles of care' and 'Other significant mental health complaints' (15.15%), 'Acute stress' (13.80%) and 'Suicide' and 'Epilepsy' (13.13%). The thematic analysis of the feedback of the PHCWs and trainers recommended the use of the guide to strengthen pre-service training and broaden the scope of the initiative to train PHCWs across the province. CONCLUSIONS: This study underscores the feasibility of implementing an adapted mhGAP-HIG for training primary care physicians and clinical psychologists within the existing healthcare resources of Khyber Pakhtunkhwa. The preliminary findings endorse the scalability across other districts in the province.
Research advances in brain-computer interfaces in oncology: from cancer pain management to neuroengineering for brain tumors and tumor-neural-immune interactions.推荐
PMID 42984888 · Frontiers in immunology · 2026 10 21
作者：Song Y, Guo H, Yu Z, Wang A et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：综述
摘要：Brain-computer interfaces (BCIs) have made significant progress in fields such as neurorehabilitation, motor reconstruction, and alternative communication by capturing, decoding, and providing feedback on brain activity. In contrast, the application of BCI in oncology has a relatively late start, but its development path has gradually become clearer in recent years. On the one hand, brain-signal interfaces based on neural feedback and closed-loop regulation have begun to be used for interventions targeting cancer-related neurological symptoms, particularly cancer pain and chemotherapy-induced peripheral neuropathy (CIPN), suggesting that supportive cancer care is shifting from empirical symptomatic management toward quantifiable, feedback-based, and adjustable neural state management. On the other hand, in the field of brain tumors, ECoG recording, high-density neural recording, and real-time decoding are providing complementary information on abnormal peritumoral networks and tumor-associated epilepsy within established function-guided surgical workflows. These recording and decision-support approaches are related to BCI engineering but are not automatically BCIs or substitutes for direct electrical stimulation mapping. More importantly, advances in tumor neuroscience suggest that neural activity not only reflects the disruption of host brain tissue by the tumor but may also participate in glioma growth and invasion through circuit remodeling and microenvironmental changes. This article provides a review of the fundamentals of BCI technology, the management of cancer-related symptoms, brain-computer interfaces in brain tumors, tumor neuroscience, tumor-neural-immune interactions, and their translational challenges. Current evidence suggests that the significance of BCI in oncology is no longer limited to assistive devices but is evolving into a novel engineering platform that integrates symptom control, surgical decision-making, and research into disease mechanisms.
POEMS syndrome mimicking diabetic peripheral neuropathy: a case report and literature review.推荐
PMID 42984680 · Frontiers in medicine · 2026 10 21
作者：Chen Y, Fang C, Liang S, Xie X et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：病例报告
摘要：POEMS syndrome is a rare multisystem plasma cell disorder characterized by peripheral neuropathy and a broad spectrum of systemic manifestations. Because peripheral neuropathy may coexist with common metabolic disorders, POEMS syndrome can be easily overlooked or misdiagnosed. We report the case of a 64-year-old woman with a 2-year history of diabetes mellitus who presented with progressive numbness of all four limbs for more than 11 months and fatigue for approximately 1 year. She was initially diagnosed with diabetic peripheral neuropathy and treated with glucose-lowering and neuroprotective therapy, but her neurological symptoms persisted and progressed. Further evaluation demonstrated demyelinating peripheral neuropathy with axonal involvement, an IgA-λ monoclonal protein, a clonal plasma cell population in the bone marrow, markedly elevated plasma vascular endothelial growth factor (VEGF), bilateral papilledema, lymphadenopathy, lower-extremity edema, and characteristic skin changes. Based on the combination of mandatory, major, and minor diagnostic features, POEMS syndrome was diagnosed. The patient initially received lenalidomide plus dexamethasone (RD), but lenalidomide was discontinued after the development of a generalized inflammatory erythematous rash with scaling and pruritus during the second treatment cycle. Treatment was subsequently changed to daratumumab, bortezomib, and dexamethasone (Dara + VD). During Dara + VD treatment, serum M-protein became undetectable, plasma VEGF decreased from 842.29 pg/mL before treatment to 836 pg/mL after the first cycle, and edema, fatigue, abdominal distension, and peripheral neuropathic symptoms improved. However, the patient was discharged after receiving only the first administration of the fourth treatment cycle. Three days after discharge, she developed generalized seizures with loss of consciousness and subsequently experienced severe systemic complications, including pneumonia, sepsis, septic shock, renal failure, and heart failure. She eventually died of multiple organ dysfunction syndrome. This case highlights the diagnostic challenge of POEMS syndrome in patients with pre-existing diabetes mellitus. Progressive or atypical peripheral neuropathy that is disproportionate to the presumed metabolic disorder should prompt evaluation for monoclonal plasma cell disorders and POEMS syndrome, particularly when accompanied by edema, skin changes, lymphadenopathy, papilledema, or elevated VEGF. In this patient, Dara + VD was associated with a short-term hematological and clinical response after lenalidomide intolerance; however, the subsequent fatal course precludes conclusions regarding its long-term efficacy.
Impaired Kv4.2-DPP6 complex dynamics in a mouse model of Angelman syndrome.推荐
PMID 42984511 · Frontiers in cellular neuroscience · 2026 10 21
作者：Hu JH, Liu Y, Malloy C, Hoffman DA
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：INTRODUCTION: Angelman syndrome (AS) is a neurodevelopmental disorder caused by loss of neuronal expression of the E3 ubiquitin ligase UBE3A and is associated with altered neuronal excitability. We previously identified the dendritic A-type K+ channel subunit Kv4.2 as an activity-dependent substrate of UBE3A. However, where UBE3A interacts with Kv4.2 during activity-dependent trafficking and how this pathway regulates Kv4.2 channel complexes and dendritic distribution remain unclear.. METHODS: We combined live-cell bungarotoxin labeling and biochemical surface-labeling approaches to examine UBE3A association with surface-derived Kv4.2. Co-immunoprecipitation and immunoblotting were used to assess Kv4.2 association with the auxiliary subunit DPP6 and activity-dependent Kv4.2 phosphorylation in wild-type (WT), UBE3A-deficient (AS), and DPP6 knockout (KO) mice. Kv4.2 immunostaining and quantitative line-scan analysis were used to examine its proximal-to-distal distribution in hippocampal CA1 dendrites. RESULTS: UBE3A preferentially associated with internalized rather than surface-resident Kv4.2, supporting a post-endocytic site of UBE3A action. In AS mice, Kv4.2-DPP6 association was reduced and failed to undergo further activity-dependent remodeling following pentylenetetrazole (PTZ)-induced seizures, despite unchanged DPP6 expression and preserved basal Kv4.2 phosphorylation. In contrast, DPP6 deficiency increased basal Kv4.2 phosphorylation at T602 and T607 and disrupted its activity-dependent phosphorylation dynamics, indicating reciprocal regulation between Kv4.2 phosphorylation and DPP6 association. DPP6KO mice exhibited impaired Kv4.2 gradients along both apical and basal CA1 dendrites. Similarly, AS mice showed impaired dendritic Kv4.2 gradients and reduced activity-dependent redistribution following kainic acid-induced seizures. CONCLUSION: These findings support a model in which neuronal activity promotes remodeling and internalization of Kv4.2-containing channel complexes, followed by UBE3A-dependent processing of the post-endocytic Kv4.2 pool. Loss of UBE3A disrupts Kv4.2-DPP6 complex dynamics and dendritic Kv4.2 organization, thereby reducing the capacity for activity-dependent channel redistribution. These results identify UBE3A as an important regulator of Kv4.2 homeostasis and provide a mechanistic link between UBE3A deficiency, altered dendritic ion-channel organization, and neuronal dysfunction in Angelman syndrome.
Impairment of ictal consciousness and psychosis in drug-resistant focal epilepsy: A retrospective clinical cohort study.推荐
PMID 42984305 · Epilepsia · 2026 11 05
作者：Tarrada A, Hingray C, Aron O, Galmiche C et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：OBJECTIVE: Psychoses of epilepsy (POE) affect up to 13% of patients with focal epilepsy. Whereas interictal risk factors are well documented, ictal correlates of POE remain poorly characterized. We hypothesized that an impairment of ictal consciousness on the Consciousness Seizure Scale (CSS), would be associated with POE. We assessed the association between ictal clinical features and POE compared to non-POE in adults with drug-resistant focal epilepsy. METHODS: We retrospectively analyzed a presurgical cohort of adults with drug-resistant focal epilepsy monitored by video-electroencephalography (EEG) or stereo-EEG. For each patient, all seizures with an examination allowing the completion of CSS score were included. POE were identified through standardized psychiatric interviews. Association between ictal features, primarily CSS score, and POE was tested using generalized estimating equations (GEE). RESULTS: Among 232 included seizures (25 from POE group, 207 from non-POE group), the probability of POE varied nonlinearly with ictal consciousness; it peaked at intermediate CSS scores (GEE, OR = 4.31, p = .006). This effect remained after adjustment for duration of both epilepsy (OR=1.06, p = .005) and seizure (OR=1.20, p = .013). Intraclass correlation of CSS was .76 (95%CI, .65-.85), reflecting a low within-patient variance. An older age, more severe depressive and anxiety symptoms were also associated with POE. SIGNIFICANCE: POE were associated with intermediate impairment of ictal consciousness, suggesting that partial disruption of awareness may favor the emergence of psychotic symptoms. The high level of intrapatient stability for CSS, mostly estimated from non-POE, suggests that CSS could be constant across seizures. This raises the possibility that POE risk could stem from the way consciousness is altered during seizures.
Longitudinal serum and CSF cytokine dynamics in NORSE: Associations with immunotherapy exposure.推荐
PMID 42984304 · Epilepsia · 2026 11 05
作者：Hanin A, Marois C, Cosme L, Calonge Q et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：OBJECTIVE: To characterize serum and cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) cytokine dynamics according to immunotherapy exposure in cryptogenic new-onset refractory status epilepticus (cNORSE). METHODS: We analyzed cytokine profiles in 94 serum and 42 CSF samples from 29 and 14 patients, respectively, with cNORSE. Nine contributed both sample types, resulting in 34 patients. Samples were categorized according to immunotherapy exposure as no immunotherapy (NT), first-line (FL), and second-line (SL), and stratified by innate- or adaptive-directed therapies. SL represented cumulative exposure, as all SL samples followed initiation of both FL and SL therapies. Linear mixed-effects models accounted for repeated measures and time from cNORSE onset. A systematic literature review included 27 studies and 63 patients. RESULTS: Serum cytokine levels were generally lower during FL (n = 24) than NT (n = 9), whereas most cytokines were higher during SL (n = 61) than FL. CXCL8 (C-X-C motif ligand 8)/IL-8 and CCL2 (C-C motif chemokine ligand 2) were higher during SL than FL, with CCL2 remaining significant after multiple-testing correction. No serum differences remained significant when analyses were restricted to samples collected 7-30 days after onset. CSF levels were generally lower during FL (n = 10) and SL (n = 25) than NT (n = 7). Innate-directed therapies were associated with higher serum IL-6, CXCL8/IL-8, and CCL2 and CSF IL-4, whereas adaptive-directed therapies were associated with lower CSF MIP-1α (Macrophage Inflammatory Protein-1 alpha). The association with higher serum IL-6 was no longer observed after excluding tocilizumab-treated patients. No temporal association after SL initiation or status epilepticus resolution remained significant after multiple-testing correction. The literature review showed more frequent reductions in pro-inflammatory cytokines in CSF than serum following immunotherapy initiation. SIGNIFICANCE: Cytokine profiles in cNORSE differ by treatment exposure and compartment. Persistent serum inflammation during SL may reflect disease severity, systemic inflammation, treatment effects, or their combination, whereas CSF showed more consistent reductions after immunotherapy exposure. These findings highlight the importance of treatment timing and compartment when interpreting cytokine measurements and support prospective studies integrating baseline immune phenotypes and standardized clinical outcomes.

作者：Sáez González M, Kloosterhuis K, Vreijling J, Mei H et al.

命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：OBJECTIVE: Rare de novo variants in GNAO1 cause a severe neurodevelopmental disorder characterized by a heterogenous display of early onset epilepsy, movement abnormalities, and global developmental delay. More than 100 pathogenic variants in GNAO1 have been identified and categorized into four groups according to their protein features. Most pathogenic variants affect both GNAO1 isoforms, whereas approximately 20% of patients have a GNAO1A-specific variant. Response to therapy is notoriously variable, even among patients harboring the same variant. Here, we set out to model GNAO1A-related encephalopathy using human induced pluripotent stem cells (hiPSCs) from monozygotic twins carrying the Gαo1 K278del variant and uncovered-while taking advantage of the identical genomic setting-phenotypic features that further our understanding of the defects underlying the disorder. Variant-specific transcriptomic, morphological, and electrophysiological characteristics were analyzed. RESULTS: Gαo1 K278del mutated hiPSC-derived NPCs showed disorganized neural rosette structures and revealed an altered transcriptome. In cortical neurons, the Gαo1 K278del variant caused a reduction in the size and complexity of dendrites that was accompanied by an increase in pre- and postsynaptic puncta. Multielectrode array (MEA) recordings revealed an abnormal cortical neuron bursting activity pattern with fewer bursts and more spikes per burst. The antiepileptic drug levetiracetam reduced the spike frequency and the number of spikes per burst in cortical neurons from both patients. SIGNIFICANCE: The developed GNAO1A hiPSC-derived in vitro model uncovered key structural and functional features of GNAO1-related encephalopathy. The comparable in vitro phenotypes in neurons from both twins underscore the utility of hiPSC-derived models to investigate molecular mechanisms underlying GNAO1-related encephalopathy as well as effects of therapeutic intervention.
Implementation of the seizure code in a tertiary hospital: Effects on emergency care times and clinical outcomes in severe seizures.推荐
PMID 42984267 · Epilepsia open · 2026 11 05
作者：Malaret-Segurado M, Ortega-Macho J, Valls-Carbó A, Parejo-Carbonell B et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：OBJECTIVE: To assess the impact of implementing the Seizure Code (SC), a structured multidisciplinary emergency protocol for severe seizures, on care times and clinical outcomes at a tertiary hospital. METHODS: Single-center, retrospective observational study including episodes of severe seizures in patients aged 18 years or older managed between January 2023 and December 2024, comparing pre-SC and post-SC periods. The primary outcome was time from seizure onset to administration of the first anti-seizure medication (ASM). Secondary outcomes included other care intervals, process indicators, and clinical outcomes. Care intervals were analyzed with accelerated failure time models (percentage change in median time), length of stay in a competing-risks framework, and binary outcomes with Firth-penalized logistic regression (odds ratios, OR), all adjusted for prespecified covariates and clustered by patient. RESULTS: Of 1959 episodes identified, 378 (175 pre-SC, 203 post-SC) met SC activation criteria. Time to the first ASM, the primary outcome, did not differ significantly (27% shorter, 95% CI -15% to 53% for benzodiazepines; 27%, -6% to 50% for others). Time to urgent video-EEG (VEEG) was 48% shorter (29%-61%) and the neurological assessment-VEEG interval 63% shorter (31%-80%). Pre-notification by Emergency Medical Services increased (22.9% vs. 37.8%; p = 0.003). Hospital stay (7.48 vs. 5.08 days; subdistribution hazard ratio for discharge alive 1.36, 1.02-1.89) and intensive care unit stay (3.20 vs. 1.48 days) were shorter. Thirty-day seizure recurrence was lower (8.0% vs. 3.0%; adjusted OR 0.33, 0.12-0.84). Mortality and functional status at discharge did not differ. SIGNIFICANCE: The primary outcome, time to first anti-seizure medication, was not significantly shorter after implementation of the structured emergency protocol. The observed benefits were concentrated in secondary process and resource-utilization indicators: implementation of the Seizure Code was associated with faster access to urgent VEEG, shorter hospital and intensive care unit stays, and lower 30-day seizure recurrence, without a significant effect on mortality. These findings support protocolized emergency pathways in severe seizures. PLAIN LANGUAGE SUMMARY: Severe seizures are emergencies in which fast, coordinated care matters. We compared how patients were treated at one hospital before and after a new protocol, the Seizure Code, was introduced. Afterwards, patients had urgent video-EEG (a recording of brain activity) sooner and spent fewer days in hospital and in the intensive care unit, and fewer had another seizure within 30 days. However, the time to the first anti-seizure medication did not change significantly, and deaths and disability at discharge were unchanged.
Treatment patterns, strategies, and preferences after first anti-seizure medication failure in focal epilepsy: Findings from the SANAD and SANAD II trial cohorts and a survey of UK epilepsy specialists.推荐
PMID 42984249 · Epilepsia · 2026 11 04
作者：Egesa IJ, Marson AG, Bonnett LJ, Emsley R et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：OBJECTIVE: To describe treatment decisions after first anti-seizure medication (ASM) failure in focal epilepsy and describe how they have changed across two trial eras, and whether observed prescribing matched clinicians stated preferences. METHODS: We analyzed the focal epilepsy cohorts from two UK pragmatic, randomized trials, Standard and New Antiepileptic Drugs (SANAD) (1999-2006) and SANAD II (2012-2019). Only initial ASM allocation was randomized; subsequent decisions reflected usual care, with medication changes and reason recorded prospectively. We reconstructed treatment sequences, classified first decisions after failure as switch or add-on, and stratified by reason, and compared prescribing with cross-sectional survey of UK epilepsy specialists. RESULTS: Of those enrolled, 1644 of 1721 (95.5%) in SANAD and 933 of 990 (94.2%) in SANAD II were included. First-line ASM failed in 798 (48.5%) and 360 (38.6%), respectively. Among classifiable failures from inadequate seizure control (ISC), add-on was 40.9% (79/193) in SANAD and 63.1% (82/130) in SANAD II. After lamotrigine failure, the only randomized ASM in both trials, add-on was 44.4% (16/36) in SANAD to 74.3% (26/35) in SANAD II. Forty-six specialists completed the survey. Their preferred next ASM broadly matched SANAD II prescribing: after lamotrigine failure from ISC, 74% (31/42) chose levetiracetam, prescribed to 66% (23/35); after levetiracetam failure, 51% (21/41) chose lamotrigine, prescribed to 52% (26/50). Strategy preferences differed: respondents favored switching after each specified failure, yet add-on predominated after ISC in SANAD II. SIGNIFICANCE: Add-on treatment after first ASM failure from ISC was more common in the later trial. Clinician-preferred next ASMs were consistent with observed practice, but preferred strategy did not. Neither trial was designed to compare these strategies, and cohorts differed in baseline characteristics and completeness of recorded reason for failure. Randomized evidence is needed to determine whether switching or add-on yields better seizure control, tolerability, and treatment-retention outcomes.
Integrating uncertainty and practice variability for epilepsy prognostication after intracranial EEG: A meta-analysis.推荐
PMID 42984217 · Epilepsia · 2026 11 04
作者：Evans M, Khoudari H, Tobochnik S, Burneo JG et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：OBJECTIVE: Intracranial electroencephalography (iEEG) practices and diagnostic testing vary among institutions, limiting standardization and optimization of surgical outcomes. This study estimates resection rates and surgical outcome effect sizes after iEEG and develops individual patient-level data (IPD) models using the missing-indicator method to approximate real-world clinical uncertainties and practice variability in iEEG and surgical decision-making. METHODS: Databases were queried for studies reporting ≥12-month outcomes after epilepsy surgery in patients with drug-resistant epilepsy (DRE) who underwent iEEG. Random-effects meta-analysis estimated pooled outcome proportions. IPD analysis was performed using multivariable generalized linear regression and logistic regression models with cross-validation and calibration assessment. Missing values for key predictors were retained as a "not specified" category to evaluate the impact of incorporating uncertainty in common clinical data on outcome prediction. RESULTS: Primary outcomes were seizure freedom (Engel class IA or International League Against Epilepsy [ILAE] 1) and favorable seizure outcomes (Engel I or ILAE 1-2). Of 4975 studies identified, 115 met the inclusion criteria. The pooled frequency of patients undergoing resection after iEEG was 67% (95% confidence interval [CI] 60%-74%), 52% achieved seizure freedom (95% CI 48%-56%), and 58% achieved a favorable outcome (95% CI 54%-62%). IPD analysis included 1305 patients from 29 studies and identified a lesional magnetic resonance imaging (MRI; odds ratio [OR] 1.94, CI 1.48-2.55, p < .0001) and a cortical malformation (OR 1.47, CI 1.08-2.00, p = .01) as independent predictors of a favorable outcome, whereas iEEG implantation strategy (bilateral implantation OR .61, 95% CI .42-.89, p = .01) was an independent predictor of a less favorable outcome. SIGNIFICANCE: About two-thirds of patients undergoing iEEG proceeded to resection, and about half achieved seizure freedom. Multivariable models incorporating clinical uncertainty and practice variability were well calibrated. However, modest discrimination suggests that routinely available clinical variables alone have limited ability to predict individual surgical outcomes. More standardized and granular clinical and practice-related data may improve individualized prognostication after iEEG.
Yield of epileptiform abnormalities and predictors on routine electroencephalography in a neurology facility in Ghana.推荐
PMID 42983630 · BMC neurology · 2026 08 07
作者：Aklamanu BW, Sefa CO, Tetteh EK, Laari JT
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：BACKGROUND: Electroencephalography (EEG) is an essential neurodiagnostic tool for the evaluation of epilepsy and other neurological disorders. However, evidence on the yield of epileptiform abnormalities of routine EEG and its predictors in sub-Saharan Africa remains limited. OBJECTIVE: To determine the yield of epileptiform abnormalities of routine EEG and identify factors associated with epileptiform discharges in a Ghanaian tertiary referral centre. METHODS: This study included 124 patients who underwent routine outpatient EEG between October 2023 and January 2026. Demographic, clinical, medication, and EEG characteristics were collected using a standardized data extraction form. The primary outcome was the presence of epileptiform discharges. Associations between patient characteristics and epileptiform activity were assessed using Chi-square and Fisher's exact tests. Multivariable logistic regression was performed to identify independent predictors, with results reported as adjusted odds ratios (aORs) and 95% confidence intervals (CIs). RESULTS: The mean age of participants was 29.4 ± 19.7 years, and 55.6% were male. Epileptiform discharges were identified in 72 patients, yielding an overall epileptiform abnormality of 58.1%. Generalized epilepsy accounted for 83.3% of epileptiform abnormalities. The highest yield was observed among children aged 0-5 years (83.3%) and adults aged ≥ 60 years (75.0%). On bivariate analysis, generalized slowing (90.9%, p = 0.025) and focal slowing (100.0%, p < 0.001) were significantly associated with epileptiform discharges. In multivariable analysis, abnormal EEG background activity remained the only independent predictor of epileptiform discharges (aOR = 23.47, 95% CI: 2.95-186.86, p = 0.003). CONCLUSION: Routine EEG demonstrated a high yield of epileptiform abnormalities in this Ghanaian tertiary centre. Abnormal EEG background activity was a strong independent predictor of epileptiform discharges, highlighting the importance of careful background assessment during EEG interpretation and supporting the continued use of routine EEG in epilepsy evaluation.
Characteristics of Emergency Department Visits by Adults With Down Syndrome: A Single-Institution Retrospective Perspective.浏览
PMID 42983603 · Academic emergency medicine : official journal of the Society for Academic Emergency Medicine · 2026 Nov
作者：Hunt EA, Johnson KF, Lucus EJ, Neiger M et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：BACKGROUND: Down syndrome, also known as Trisomy 21, is the most common chromosomal abnormality causing intellectual disability. The life expectancy for people with Down syndrome has increased considerably over the last few decades, and many of these patients struggle with the transition from pediatric to adult medical care. There is currently no information on how adults with Down syndrome use emergency departments (EDs). The objective of this study was to describe visits by adult patients with Down syndrome to an academic medical center. METHODS: This was a retrospective chart review study of all adult patients (age ≥ 18 years) with Down syndrome who presented to The Ohio State University Wexner Medical Center (OSUWMC) EDs from January 1, 2019 to December 31, 2023. Selected patient and visit characteristics were collected by chart review. This was a descriptive study with data presented as n and percentage. RESULTS: There were 177 ED visits by 106 adults with Down syndrome during the study period. Most patients had a single ED visit (69.8%) and more than half the patients were under age 40 (52.9%). The most common chief complaints were abdominal pain (10.2%) and seizure (7.9%). The majority received laboratory testing (82.5%) and/or imaging (74.6%) This pattern was present across the age span. Most were discharged to place of residence (51.4%) but admission rates were higher (38.4%). Laboratory testing, imaging and admission rates were higher compared to local and national rates. CONCLUSIONS: This description of ED visits by adult patients with Down syndrome provides insight into common presenting complaints, testing patterns, disposition, and post-ED visits. Study data suggest that adult patients with Down syndrome may require different considerations when they present to adult EDs compared to a typical ED patient. Further studies are needed to generalize these results and identify ways to improve their ED care.
Coffin-Siris Syndrome推荐
PMID 23556151 · GeneReviews® · 2013-4-4（更新 2026-11-5）
作者：Schrier Vergano S, Santen G, Wieczorek D, Matsumoto N
链接：PubMed
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：图书章节/更新
摘要：CLINICAL CHARACTERISTICS: Classically, Coffin-Siris syndrome (CSS) was characterized by specific dysmorphic features (coarse facies, sparse scalp hair, thick eyebrows with long lashes, wide nasal bridge with broad nasal tip, anteverted nares with thick ala nasi, wide mouth with thick, everted vermilion of the upper and lower lips, and hypertrichosis), the absence or underdevelopment of the fifth digit finger/toe or nail, learning and developmental differences, and various organ system-related anomalies. As genetic technology has evolved, more individuals with subtle physical exam findings are being diagnosed with CSS. The vast majority of affected individuals have developmental delay / intellectual disability, typically in the moderate-to-severe range, although those with mild cognitive impairment or even normal intelligence have more rarely been described. Most affected individuals have feeding difficulties (with ~25%-50% requiring a feeding tube in childhood, some of whom then outgrow it), hypotonia, and frequent infections. About half of affected individuals have epilepsy. Other findings may include skeletal features (joint laxity, scoliosis), hearing impairment (both conductive and sensorineural), eye issues (ptosis, strabismus), congenital heart defects, genitourinary malformations, and behavioral issues (including hyperactivity and/or aggressiveness). DIAGNOSIS/TESTING: The diagnosis of CSS is established in a proband with suggestive findings and a heterozygous pathogenic variant in one of the following 14 known genes identified by molecular genetic testing: ARID1A, ARID1B, ARID2, BICRA, DPF2, PHF6, SMARCA2, SMARCA4, SMARCB1, SMARCC2, SMARCD1, SMARCE1, SOX4, or SOX11. MANAGEMENT: Treatment of manifestations: Feeding therapy with consideration of placement of gastrostomy tube in those with persistent feeding issues. Standard treatment for developmental delay / intellectual disability, epilepsy, tics, sleep disturbance, scoliosis, joint laxity, knee subluxations, obesity, refractive error, strabismus, ptosis, hearing loss, congenital heart defects, undervirilization, inguinal hernia, frequent infections, and hepatoblastoma. Surveillance: At each visit, measurement of growth parameters; evaluation of nutrition status and safety of oral intake; assessment for new neurologic manifestations, such as seizures or tics; monitoring of developmental progress and educational needs; behavioral assessment for anxiety, ADHD, ASD, aggression, & self-injury; monitoring for signs and symptoms of sleep disturbance; evaluation for mobility and self-help skills; and assessment for signs and symptoms of frequent infections. Annually or as clinically indicated, ophthalmology evaluation and vision assessment; audiology evaluation. At least every six months in those with teeth, dental evaluation. In those with ARID1A-related CSS: serum AFP level (with consideration of liver ultrasound) every three months until age four years. GENETIC COUNSELING: CSS is inherited in an autosomal dominant manner; however, most affected individuals have the disorder as the result of a de novo CSS-causing pathogenic variant. If the CSS-causing pathogenic variant has been identified in an affected family member, prenatal and preimplantation genetic testing are possible.
Allan-Herndon-Dudley Syndrome推荐
PMID 20301789 · GeneReviews® · 2010-3-9（更新 2026-11-5）
作者：Sarret C, Oliver Petit I, Tonduti D
链接：PubMed
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：图书章节/更新
摘要：CLINICAL CHARACTERISTICS: Allan-Herndon-Dudley syndrome (AHDS), an X-linked disorder, is characterized in males by neurologic findings (hypotonia and feeding difficulties in infancy, developmental delay / intellectual disability ranging from mild to profound) and later-onset pyramidal signs, extrapyramidal findings (dystonia, choreoathetosis, paroxysmal movement disorder, hypokinesia, masked facies), and seizures, often with drug resistance. Additional findings can include dysthyroidism (manifest as poor weight gain, reduced muscle mass, and variable cold intolerance, sweating, elevated heart rate, and irritability) and pathognomonic thyroid test results. Most heterozygous females are not clinically affected but may have minor thyroid test abnormalities. DIAGNOSIS/TESTING: The diagnosis of AHDS is established in a male proband with suggestive findings and a hemizygous SLC16A2 pathogenic variant identified by molecular genetic testing, and in a female proband with a heterozygous pathogenic variant in SLC16A2 identified by molecular genetic testing. MANAGEMENT: Targeted therapy: Tiratricol (Emcitate®) for the treatment of peripheral thyrotoxicosis in adults and children with AHDS. Supportive care: Multidisciplinary team to provide standard care for hypotonia, poor feeding, DD/ID, spasticity, and extrapyramidal movement disorders. Standard treatment with anti-seizure medication by an experienced neurologist. Thyroid hormone replacement therapy during childhood has no beneficial effect and could be dangerous by worsening peripheral thyrotoxicosis. Surveillance: In children, assess the following every six months until age four years, then once a year: developmental progress & educational needs; neurologic examination for new manifestations (e.g., seizures, changes in tone, movement disorders); spine for scoliosis and hips for dislocation; mobility and self-help skills; signs and symptoms of peripheral thyrotoxicosis (tachycardia, high blood pressure, intestinal problems). Obtain a DXA scan and phosphocalcic equilibrium annually in non-ambulatory individuals. In those on tiratricol therapy, obtain serum free T3, free T4, total T4, and SHBG every two weeks during treatment titration, then every three months as soon as free T3 normalizes. Serum levels should be obtained on a morning sample before the morning dose of tiratricol is given and preferably by mass spectrometry for T3 level, as this avoids interference with measurement of the residual drug. Agents/circumstances to avoid: Administration of L-T4 or L-T3 alone can exacerbate the high serum T3 levels and the resulting hypermetabolism. GENETIC COUNSELING: AHDS is inherited in an X-linked manner. If the mother of a proband has an SLC16A2 pathogenic variant, the chance of transmitting it in each pregnancy is 50%. Males who inherit the pathogenic variant will be affected; females who inherit the variant will be heterozygotes (carriers) and usually will not be clinically affected but may have minor thyroid test abnormalities. Once the SLC16A2 pathogenic variant has been identified in an affected family member, carrier testing of at-risk female relatives and prenatal/preimplantation genetic testing are possible.
Microphthalmia with Linear Skin Defects Syndrome推荐
PMID 20301767 · GeneReviews® · 2009-6-18（更新 2026-11-5）
作者：Morleo M, D'Alessio AM, Franco B
链接：PubMed
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：图书章节/更新
摘要：CLINICAL CHARACTERISTICS: Microphthalmia with linear skin defects (MLS) syndrome is characterized by unilateral or bilateral microphthalmia and/or anophthalmia and linear skin defects, usually involving the face and neck, which are present at birth and heal with age, leaving minimal residual scarring. Other findings can include a wide variety of other ocular abnormalities (e.g., corneal anomalies, orbital cysts, cataracts), central nervous system involvement (e.g., structural anomalies, developmental delay, infantile seizures), cardiac manifestations (e.g., atrial or ventricular septal defects, arrhythmias, hypertrophic or oncocytic cardiomyopathy), short stature, urogenital anomalies, diaphragmatic hernia, hearing impairment, and nail dystrophy. Inter- and intrafamilial variability is described. DIAGNOSIS/TESTING: The diagnosis of MLS syndrome can be established in a proband with two major criteria (microphthalmia and/or anophthalmia AND linear skin defects); or in a proband with a heterozygous Xp22.2 deletion involving HCCS or a heterozygous or hemizygous pathogenic variant in COX7B, HCCS, or NDUFB11 identified by molecular genetic testing. MANAGEMENT: Treatment of manifestations: Use of a prosthesis under the guidance of an oculoplastics specialist for severe microphthalmia and anophthalmia; treatment of other ocular findings per ophthalmic subspecialist; low vision services; routine dermatologic care for significant skin lesions; treatment of seizures and/or other neurologic abnormalities by a pediatric neurologist; developmental therapies and special education as indicated for developmental delay and intellectual disability; routine care for other medical concerns when present. Surveillance: Monitoring and follow up with ophthalmologist, dermatologist, pediatric neurologist, developmental pediatrician, cardiologist, and other professionals as needed. GENETIC COUNSELING: MLS syndrome is an X-linked disorder typically caused by a de novo genetic alteration. Rarely, a female proband has the disorder as the result of a genetic alteration inherited from her mother, who may or may not be affected. MLS syndrome is usually lethal in males. Live-born affected males typically have the disorder as the result of a de novo chromosomal rearrangement; one live-born affected male with a normal chromosome analysis and a pathogenic variant in an MLS syndrome-related gene has been reported to date. If the mother of the proband is affected and/or is known to have an MLS syndrome-related genetic alteration, the chance of transmitting the genetic alteration at conception is 50%. Because male conceptuses who inherit such a genetic alteration are usually nonviable, the likelihood of a live-born affected child is less than 50%. Females who inherit an MLS syndrome-related genetic alteration may have no apparent features of the disorder or have a range of clinical manifestations. Once the MLS syndrome-related genetic alteration has been identified in an affected family member, identification of female heterozygotes and prenatal/preimplantation genetic testing are possible.
Drug-Drug Interactions of Cenobamate With Medications: Dose-Dependent Induction/Inhibition and Clinical Implications.浏览
PMID 42982766 · CPT: pharmacometrics & systems pharmacology · 2026 Nov
作者：Ichida H, Nakao T, Mori T, Makino R et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：Cenobamate causes dose-dependent cytochrome P450 (CYP)2B6 and CYP3A4 induction and CYP2C19 inhibition, raising concern over multi-pathway drug-drug interactions (DDIs) in patients with epilepsy treated with cardiovascular co-medications. We developed a physiologically based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) model for cenobamate and verified it using reported DDIs for cenobamate 200 mg using three probe substrates: bupropion (CYP2B6), midazolam (CYP3A4), and omeprazole (CYP2C19). The verified model predicted exposure changes (area under the curve ratio [AUCR] and maximum concentration [Cmax] ratio with/without cenobamate) for eight drugs, including anticoagulants and antiplatelets (apixaban, cilostazol, clopidogrel, and rivaroxaban), and antihypertensives (amlodipine, bisoprolol, carvedilol, and nifedipine). Regarding anticoagulants and antiplatelets, for apixaban, AUCR/Cmax ratios were 0.78/0.92 (100 mg), 0.71/0.87 (200 mg), and 0.63/0.83 (400 mg), respectively. For cilostazol, parent exposure decreased (0.67/0.85, 0.55/0.77, 0.45/0.70), while the active metabolite OPC-13015 was predicted to increase in Cmax. For clopidogrel, parent exposure was unchanged, but formation of 2-oxo-clopidogrel and clopidogrel-H4 were decreased and increased, respectively. For rivaroxaban, the AUCR/Cmax ratios were 0.78/0.90, 0.69/0.85, and 0.61/0.80. Among antihypertensives, amlodipine showed decreased AUCR/Cmax ratios (0.57/0.66, 0.45/0.55, 0.37/0.47). The exposure changes of bisoprolol were minor (AUCR 0.84-0.92, Cmax ratio 0.95-0.98); AUCR/Cmax ratios of carvedilol fell to 0.73/0.80, 0.63/0.71, and 0.55/0.64; and nifedipine decreased markedly (0.41/0.54, 0.29/0.41, 0.21/0.32). In conclusion, cenobamate induced CYP2B6 weakly and CYP3A4 moderately, and it inhibited CYP2C19 moderately to drive dose-dependent decreases in exposure for seven of eight drugs with minimal change for bisoprolol. The verified PBPK framework enables risk assessment in scenarios not yet clinically tested.
[A case of bilateral open-lip schizencephaly with West syndrome due to variant of PAFAH1B1 gene and literature review].推荐
PMID 42982429 · Zhonghua yi xue yi chuan xue za zhi = Zhonghua yixue yichuanxue zazhi = Chinese journal of medical genetics · 2026 Oct 10
作者：Liu X, Ma H, Liu P, Gao M et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：综述
摘要：OBJECTIVE: To report the clinical manifestations, genetic features, diagnosis, treatment, and prognosis of a child with bilateral open-lip schizencephaly complicated by West syndrome due to a variant of PAFAH1B1 gene, and review the relevant literature. METHODS: Clinical data of a 4-month-old boy were retrospectively analyzed, and 35 previously reported cases were systematically reviewed. This study was approved by the Medical Ethics Committee of Gansu Provincial People's Hospital (Ethics No.: 2025-871). RESULTS: The 4-month-old boy presented with clustered flexor spasms, hypsarrhythmia on electroencephalography, and developmental regression. Brain magnetic resonance imaging revealed bilateral pachygyria, schizencephaly, and dysgenesis of the corpus callosum. Trio whole-exome sequencing identified a de novo heterozygous NM_000430.4: c.703_704delAG (p.Glu235Metfs*20) frameshift variant in the PAFAH1B1 gene. Sanger sequencing confirmed that neither parent carried this variant. Based on the guidelines from American College of Medical Genetics and Genomics (ACMG), the variant has met the criteria for PVS1+PS2_Moderate+PM2_Supporting, and was classified as pathogenic. After treatment with adrenocorticotropic hormone combined with vigabatrin and other antiseizure medications, the epileptic spasms were controlled and the electroencephalographic abnormalities had improved. However, global developmental delay persisted at the 12-month follow-up. Analysis of the present case and 35 previously reported cases showed that PAFAH1B1-related phenotypes were highly consistent, mainly including infantile spasms (28/36), developmental delay/intellectual disability (29/36), and abnormal brain MRI findings (30/36), predominantly cortical malformations. The reported variant types included deletions, frameshift variants, nonsense variants, missense variants, and splice-site variants. CONCLUSION: PAFAH1B1 gene variants are an important cause for cortical malformations, including schizencephaly, and may lead to secondary West syndrome. This study has systematically summarized the genotypic and phenotypic features of bilateral open-lip schizencephaly complicated by West syndrome associated with a frameshift variant of the PAFAH1B1 gene. For infants with epileptic spasms or early-onset epilepsy accompanied by structural brain abnormalities, early genetic evaluation should be performed, and antiseizure treatment should be integrated with neurodevelopmental rehabilitation to facilitate long-term management.
Seizure susceptibility following a single acute cluster of cortical spreading depolarizations in a rat model of traumatic brain injury.推荐
PMID 42982396 · Experimental neurology · 2026 11 04
作者：Udeozor M, DeSana AJ, Osbourne JA, Mallabo K et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：Traumatic brain injury (TBI) is a major risk factor for acquired epilepsy, yet the acute pathophysiologic events contributing to post-traumatic epileptogenesis remain poorly understood. Cortical spreading depolarizations (CSDs) occur in up to 60% of moderate-to-severe TBI patients and impose substantial metabolic and neurovascular stress on the injured brain, but their contribution to post-traumatic epilepsy (PTE) is unknown. We investigated whether acute repetitive CSDs influence epileptogenesis following experimental TBI. Adult male Sprague Dawley rats underwent a lateral fluid percussion or sham injury followed by 2 h of KCl-induced CSDs. Animals survived for 6 months with video electroencephalographic monitoring for spontaneous seizures, followed by pentylenetetrazol (PTZ) seizure susceptibility testing and histologic evaluation of hippocampal markers. Spontaneous seizures occurred in 14% of TBI animals, but we did not identify significant interaction effects of TBI and CSDs. However, TBI and TBI + CSD animals experienced seizures with increased electrographic intensity in comparison to sham animals (p = 0.0194 and p = 0.0054, respectively). No significant differences were observed in hippocampal volume (2-way ANOVA, p = 0.285), or parvalbumin (p = 0.689) or calretinin (p = 0.848) immunopositivity. In contrast, CSDs were associated with decreased zinc transporter 3 (ZnT3) immunopositivity in the hippocampal hilus (p = 0.025). These findings suggest that a single acute cluster of CSDs can promote neuronal hypersynchrony and molecular adaptations in the hippocampus, despite not significantly increasing seizure incidence. Together, our findings provide initial evidence that CSDs may modify features associated with epileptogenesis and warrant further investigation of whether greater, more prolonged CSD burdens, as observed clinically after TBI, contribute to the development of PTE.
Elevated neuronal excitability and seizure susceptibility in a mouse model of SCN2A-related developmental and epileptic encephalopathy.推荐
PMID 42982203 · Neurobiology of disease · 2026 11 04
作者：Hawkins NA, Echevarria-Cooper DM, Corbett EE, Thompson CH et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：Pathogenic variants in SCN2A cause a spectrum of neurodevelopmental disorders, including developmental and epileptic encephalopathies (DEE). The patient-associated SCN2A-p.E430A variant selectively shifts voltage-dependence of activation, a mechanism predicted to enhance neuronal excitability. To investigate the functional consequences of the SCN2A-E430A variant, we generated the novel Scn2aE430A mouse model and assessed neuronal excitability, brain activity, seizure susceptibility, and phenytoin responsiveness. Heterozygous Scn2aE430A mice retained normal Scn2a expression levels and did not exhibit premature lethality. Hippocampal pyramidal neurons from heterozygous Scn2aE430A mice were hyperexcitable compared to wild-type neurons. Enhanced long-term potentiation was observed in the CA1 circuit of Scn2aE430A mice. Video-EEG data revealed recurrent epileptiform discharges and spectral abnormalities, but no spontaneous generalized seizure events. In multiple seizure-induction assays, Scn2aE430A mice had no difference in latency to first seizure signs, but exhibited faster seizure generalization and more lethality compared to wild-type mice, indicating alterations in seizure propagation and/or cessation rather than seizure initiation. Pretreatment with phenytoin improved seizure outcomes across all seizure induction methods. Together, these findings demonstrate that selective disruption of Scn2a activation gating is sufficient to drive neuronal hyperexcitability and network dysfunction, establishing the Scn2aE430A mouse as pharmacologically tractable model for investigating epilepsy related phenotypes associated with SCN2A gain of function.
A small molecule inhibitor of human ARV1 sterol-binding protein blocks lipid binding.浏览
PMID 42982185 · The Journal of biological chemistry · 2026 11 04
作者：Liu HY, Savoly D, Stroke I, Francisco J et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：ARV1 (are2 required for viability 1) sterol-binding protein is conserved across eukaryotic species and binds cholesterol, phospholipids, and phosphoinositides. Mutations in hARV1 are linked to epileptic encephalitis. mArv1-deficient mice show resistance to diet-induced obesity. Scarv1Δ cells have defects in glycosylphosphatidylinositol biosynthesis, sterol distribution and sphingolipid biosynthesis. ARV1 knockdown in human cells leads to endoplasmic reticulum cholesterol accumulation. How ARV1 regulates lipid movement is not understood. The development of inhibitors targeting the ability of ARV1 to bind lipid will provide valuable insights into its function. Employing a homogenous time-resolved fluorescence high throughput screening assay, we identified VB-84133 as an inhibitor of human ARV1 lipid binding with an IC50 of 3.2 + 0.21 μM. VB-84133 blocks the interaction of the ARV1 homology domain (AHD) with cholesterol, phosphatidylserine and other phospholipids in liposome binding assays. This inhibition was specific to ARV1 interaction, since VB-84133 did not block binding of phosphatidylserine to Annexin A2 or A5. We demonstrated that ARV1 moves phosphatidylserine between inner and outer membranes using an in vitro fluorescence-based liposome quenching assay, and that movement was inhibited by VB-84133. In phenotypic studies, S. cerevisiae ARV1 cells undergo arv1-associated cell death in the presence of VB-84133. VB-84133 constitutively activates the unfolded protein response (UPR) and XBP1 splicing in HepG2 cells but does not synergize with loss of ARV1 gene expression in further progressing splicing. VB-84133 also inhibits NBD-cholesterol localization to lipid droplets. Overall, VB-84133 specifically targets ARV1, evidenced by its capacity to bind ARV1 and trigger loss-of-function events associated with ARV1 deficiency.
Racial and ethnic disparities in health status, health literacy, and access to care among adults with epilepsy: an exploratory mediation analysis.推荐

作者：Wang B, Carlson C, Raghavan M, Pandya VB

发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：OBJECTIVE: Racial and ethnic disparities in epilepsy outcomes are well documented, but underlying mechanisms remain incompletely understood. We examined disparities in health status, health literacy, and healthcare access among adults with epilepsy and assessed the contribution of socioeconomic status (SES). METHODS: Adults with epilepsy were identified from the All of Us Research Program Controlled Tier Curated Data Repository (v2024Q3R9) using SNOMED and OMOP concepts. Participants were categorized as non-Hispanic White (NHW), non-Hispanic Black (NHB), or Hispanic/Latino (HL). Multivariable logistic regression adjusted for age, sex, education, and income to estimate adjusted odds ratios (aORs) for health status, health literacy, and healthcare barriers. Counterfactual mediation analysis with 1,000 bootstrap simulations estimated the proportion of disparities attributable to SES. RESULTS: Among 10,095 adults with epilepsy (NHW, n = 5,690; NHB, n = 2,426; HL, n = 1,979), NHB and HL participants had greater socioeconomic disadvantage and higher unadjusted burdens of poor health, limited health literacy, and healthcare barriers. SES accounted for 50-72% of disparities in health literacy and 60-70% of disparities in severe pain. CONCLUSION: SES explains a substantial but incomplete proportion of racial and ethnic disparities among adults with epilepsy. Interventions addressing socioeconomic disadvantage, health literacy, language-concordant care, and structural barriers may help reduce persistent inequities. LOE: III.

Severe and Unexpected Events After Acute Withdrawal of Tiapride Contaminating Vigabatrin: A Case Series.推荐
PMID 42981665 · Pediatric neurology · 2026 10 13
作者：Szczepaniak K, Pielas M, Chmielewski D, Borkowska J et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：BACKGROUND: In September 2023, the Polish Ministry of Health warned that two lots of Sabril (vigabatrin) powder for oral solution and tablets, released in 2021, contained trace amounts of tiapride. METHODS AND RESULTS: We identified three children with tuberous sclerosis complex who presented in the Department of Neurology and Epileptology, Children's Memorial Health Institute with sudden clinical worsening and unexplained symptoms after withdrawal of contaminated vigabatrin lots. The reported adverse effects included increased frequency and severity of seizures, significant behavioral problems, insomnia, weight loss, and secondary amenorrhea. One patient required admission to the intensive care unit due to drug-resistant status epilepticus. CONCLUSIONS: Our observations suggest that rapid withdrawal of tiapride, even administered in low doses, may result in significant and life-threatening deterioration in patients with epilepsy.
Long-term sequelae of transient global amnesia: A systematic review and meta-analysis.推荐
PMID 42981611 · Clinical neurology and neurosurgery · 2026 10 20
作者：Halahakoon C, Li KL, Saksena RK
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：综述
摘要：BACKGROUND AND OBJECTIVES: Though largely regarded as benign, the frightening experience of transient global amnesia (TGA) raises concerns over recurrence and long-term impact for patients. We aim to provide a comprehensive systematic review and meta-analysis of the long-term sequelae of TGA regarding recurrence, cognitive, neurological and neuropsychiatric complications. METHODS: PubMed, Embase, Scopus, Cochrane, WHO ICTRP, and ClinicalTrials.gov were searched from January 1, 2000 to January 31, 2026. Eligible studies assessed recurrence, stroke, seizure/epilepsy, cognitive and neuropsychiatric outcomes post-TGA. Random-effects meta-analysis of proportions was done for recurrence, stroke and seizure/epilepsy. Lack of standardised control arms prevented relative risk analysis. RESULTS: In 14 cohort studies (n = 3365), the pooled proportion of recurrence post-TGA was 8% (95% CI, 5-12%; I2 84.6%). GRADE certainty was 'very low'. For stroke occurrence post-TGA, 4 studies (n = 852) showed a pooled proportion of 3% (95% Cl, 2-6%; I2=42.1%). GRADE certainty was 'low'. For seizure/epilepsy post-TGA, 4 studies (n2=964) provided a pooled proportion of 1% (95% Cl, 0-4%; I2 = 36.7%). GRADE certainty was 'very low'. Qualitative synthesis finds, in a small and heterogeneous dataset, a favourable prognostic profile regarding cognitive and neuropsychiatric sequelae with transient effects resolving on long-term follow-up. CONCLUSION: This meta-analysis finds that TGA has a reassuring pattern of complications. However, the low certainty of evidence indicates the current evidence base is insufficient to draw strong conclusions. Patients should be informed of low pooled proportions, but clinician advice should be tailored to individual significance of complications.
