Multinodular and vacuolating neuronal tumor: 4 histopathologically diagnosed cases and systematic review of the literature.推荐
PMID 42981465 · Journal of neuropathology and experimental neurology · 2026 11 04
作者：Carmichael A, Heinemann DM, Arshad U, Kalluri A et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：Multinodular and vacuolating neuronal tumor (MVNT) is an incompletely understood CNS neoplasm due to its recent recognition and infrequent surgical sampling. This study characterizes MVNT through an institutional case series and systematic review. Four histopathologically confirmed MVNTs were retrospectively identified from our institution, and a PRISMA-guided systematic review of PubMed, Embase, Scopus, and Web of Science for radiologically or pathologically indicated MVNTs was performed. Two institutional patients presented with seizures and T2-hyperintense nodules on MRI (2/2, 100.0%). Histology revealed vacuolated neuronal cells within vacuolated stroma, with strong OLIG2 (3/3, 100.0%) and retained ATRX (2/2, 100.0%) immunoreactivity. Next-generation sequencing identified a pathogenic MAP2K1 deletion (p.Q56_V60del) in one case. The systematic review identified 310 MVNTs across 49 studies. Frequent presentations included headaches (87/310, 28.1%) and seizures (69/310, 22.3%) in patients with T2-hyperintense lesions (289/289, 100.0%). Reported immunohistochemical findings varied, although MAP2 (12/13, 92.3%) and OLIG2 (19/29, 65.5%) were frequently positive. MAP2K1 alterations including microdeletions associated with pathway activation were the most frequent oncogenic mutations. Radiologic progression was rare (1/294, 0.3%). In conclusion, MVNTs are radiologically distinct lesions that are often discovered in individuals with headaches or seizures. Histopathology reinforces a benign glioneuronal origin, and recurrent MAPK pathway alterations suggest a shared mechanism of tumorigenesis.
Comprehensive classification of HCN1 variants linked to neurodevelopmental disorders with and without epilepsy.推荐
PMID 42981027 · Proceedings of the National Academy of Sciences of the United States of America · 2026 11 04
作者：Castelli R, Marini C, Porro A, Castellini A et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：Hyperpolarization-activated cyclic nucleotide-gated 1 (HCN1) channels mediate the cationic Ih current, a fundamental regulator of neuronal excitability and synaptic integration, and are predominantly expressed in the cortex and hippocampus. Although HCN1 variants have been increasingly identified in patients with epilepsy, from milder forms to developmental and epileptic encephalopathies (DEE), genotype-phenotype correlations remain poorly defined. Here, we used patch-clamp electrophysiology and confocal imaging in HEK293 cells to functionally characterize 43 HCN1 variants identified in patients with neurodevelopmental disorders, with or without epilepsy. Based on biophysical properties, we established a classification system comprising four distinct functional classes: I) low or no current; II) hyperpolarizing shift in voltage dependence; III) depolarizing shift in voltage dependence; and IV) generation of an instantaneous current. The molecular basis for this classification was further interpreted using available cryo-EM structures of HCN1. Integration with clinical data from 50 patients revealed a striking correlation between functional class and clinical presentation. Loss-of-function variants prevailed among individuals with milder phenotypes or without epilepsy, with the latter distinctively clustering within the channel selectivity filter. In contrast, gain-of-function and mixed variants were predominantly associated with DEE. Finally, we demonstrate that allosteric modulators, including peptides NB6 and TRIP8bnano and the small molecule J&J12e, effectively normalize channel function across three of the four classes in vitro. These findings establish a biophysical framework for interpreting HCN1 variants, demonstrating that the direction of channel dysfunction is a major determinant of epilepsy risk and severity, and providing a conceptual foundation for future class-based precision medicine approaches.
From multi-omics screening to functional validation: The SOD1-SIRT5 Axis as a potential mediator of ammonia-related neuronal injury and NAD⁺-based neuroprotection in epilepsy.推荐
PMID 42980871 · Metabolic brain disease · 2026 11 04
作者：Han J, Huang Y, Li S, Xuan C et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：While the excitation-inhibition imbalance theory has long dominated epilepsy research, the role of cerebral ammonia dysregulation remains underexplored. Herein, we integrated bioinformatics with multi-level experimental validation utilizing tissues from epilepsy patients, a kainic acid-induced chronic mouse model, and an in vitro magnesium-free neuronal model. Five core genes (SOD1, SIRT5, ALB, GFAP, and PLA2G2A) were identified to form a promising predictive model for epilepsy. Cerebral ammonia levels were significantly elevated across all models, accompanied by downregulation of SOD1, SIRT5, and NeuN, alongside significant hippocampal neuronal loss. Network pharmacology and molecular docking identified NAD⁺ as the top compound targeting both SOD1 and SIRT5. Functional studies revealed that overexpression of SOD1 or SIRT5, as well as NAD⁺ treatment, effectively reduced ammonia accumulation, increased neuronal viability, and restored NeuN expression. Notably, neither genetic overexpression nor NAD⁺ treatment directly altered baseline neuronal firing, indicating that their neuroprotective efficacy is mediated through mitigation of ammonia toxicity rather than direct suppression of hyperexcitability. Collectively, these findings suggest that cerebral ammonia dysregulation is associated with dysfunction of the SOD1-SIRT5 axis, which may contribute to AICD in epilepsy, and that restoring this axis via SOD1/SIRT5 overexpression or NAD⁺ reduces ammonia accumulation and promotes neuronal survival.
External validation of the Shankar-Krings angioarchitecture score for discrimination of seizure presentation in brain arteriovenous malformations.推荐
PMID 42980854 · Neurosurgical review · 2026 11 04
作者：Mosteiro A, Dolz G, Werner M, Roldán P et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：About 20-30% of patients with newly diagnosed unruptured arteriovenous malformations (AVMs) present with seizures. However, the mechanisms and AVM-related factors predisposing to seizure onset remain incompletely understood. To address this gap, a score to discriminate seizure presentation based on AVM haemodynamic characteristics (including venous outflow restriction, nidus location, and presence of a long pial draining vein) was recently proposed. Despite its potential clinical utility, the Shankar-Krings AVM seizure score has not yet been externally validated, thus limiting its generalizability. Herein, we aimed to validate the Shankar-Krings score in an independent patient cohort. We conducted an ambispective, single-centre cohort study including patients diagnosed with unruptured brain AVMs. Angiographic features were systematically evaluated, and variables associated with seizure presentation were assessed using multivariable analysis. The three components of the Shankar-Krings AVM seizure score were assigned values of 0 or 1 as originally described. Model discrimination was assessed by calculating the area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUC). Of 118 AVMs identified between 2013 and 2024, 45 unruptured AVMs were included in the final analysis, of which 19 (42%) presented with seizures. On multivariable analyses, location in frontal, parietal or temporal lobes remained independently associated with seizure presentation (p < 0.05). Seizure prevalence increased stepwise with increasing score values (0%, 25%, 47%, and 100%; p < 0.001). The score showed good discrimination, with an AUC of 0.845. Seizures are a frequently unrecognized presentation in unruptured AVMs, despite their substantial impact on patient's health and quality of life. As such, they may justify an active interventional treatment. In this study we externally validated a previously proposed score for seizure association in unruptured AVMs. While the three-point score appeared to be a robust and practical tool for estimating seizure risk in this patient population, its role in supporting treatment decision-making requires further prospective evaluation.
Efficacy and safety of cenobamate in the treatment of drug-resistant epilepsy: a systematic review and meta-analysis in adult, pediatric, and combined populations.推荐
PMID 42980819 · European journal of clinical pharmacology · 2026 11 04
作者：Gong X, Li Z, Shen Y, Gan J
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：Meta分析
摘要：OBJECTIVES: Cenobamate (CNB), a next-generation anti-seizure medication approved by the US Food and Drug Administration (FDA) in 2019, offers a new treatment option for adult with focal-onset seizures. However, clinical experience with CNB in children remains limited, as do comparative analyses of its efficacy between children and adults. Therefore, we conducted a meta-analysis to evaluate the efficacy and safety of CNB for treating drug-resistant epilepsy (DRE) across both age groups. METHODS: We systematically searched PubMed, Embase, Web of Science, and the Cochrane Library for studies published from inception to May 2026. Data were analyzed using RStudio with the meta, metafor, and dplyr packages. A random-effects model was used for all pooled estimates because substantial heterogeneity was anticipated and observed. Risk of bias in randomized controlled trials (RCTs) was assessed using the Cochrane Risk of Bias 2.0 tool, while the ROBINS-I (Risk Of Bias In Non-randomized Studies of Interventions) tool was used for retrospective, non-randomized studies. RESULTS: After duplicate removal, 304 records were identified. Based on prespecified inclusion and exclusion criteria, 36 studies (36 reports: 35 full-text articles and 1 conference abstract) were included in the final analysis. Of these, 22 studies comprised 6,256 adults with epilepsy, 6 studies included 256 pediatric patients, and 8 mixed-age studies involving 451 patients did not provide separable age-specific data; these studies were therefore included only in the overall (all-age) analysis and were not included in the adult or pediatric subgroup analyses. In adults, the pooled responder proportion (≥ 50% seizure reduction) was 0.65 (21 studies, 3,871 patients; 95% CI: 0.58-0.71), the seizure freedom rate was 0.15 (21 studies, 3,963 patients; 95% CI: 0.10-0.21), the incidence of adverse events (AEs) was 0.63 (17 studies, 3,611 patients; 95% CI: 0.51-0.74), and the incidence of serious adverse events (SAEs) was 0.00 (19 studies, 3,487 patients; 95% CI: 0.00-0.02). In pediatric patients, the preliminary response rate was 0.60 (6 studies, 256 patients; 95% CI: 0.39-0.78), the preliminary seizure freedom rate was 0.20 (4 studies, 212 patients; 95% CI: 0.15-0.26), the preliminary incidence of AEs was 0.48 (5 studies, 236 patients; 95% CI: 0.42-0.55), and the preliminary incidence of SAEs was 0.03 (4 studies, 63 patients; 95% CI: 0.01-0.12). These estimates were numerically similar to those observed in adults. In the overall analysis (all patients combined), the response rate was 0.63 (35 studies, 4,578 patients; 95% CI: 0.58-0.68), the seizure freedom rate was 0.15 (32 studies, 4,392 patients; 95% CI: 0.11-0.19), the incidence of AEs was 0.62 (29 studies, 4,253 patients; 95% CI: 0.53-0.69), and the incidence of SAEs was 0.00 (30 studies, 3,767 patients; 95% CI: 0.00-0.02). CONCLUSION: In summary, this is the first comprehensive meta-analysis to evaluate the efficacy and safety of CNB across pediatric, adult, and combined populations. Although pediatric point estimates were numerically similar to adult estimates, these findings are derived from indirect subgroup comparisons and should be interpreted cautiously. Most included studies were retrospective observational studies with notably small pediatric samples, inherently limiting the strength of evidence in children. Therefore, further prospective studies are warranted to better investigate the efficacy and safety of CNB specifically in pediatric patients.
Advances in Multidimensional Molecular Mechanisms of Epilepsy and Precision Therapy Strategies.推荐
PMID 42980462 · CNS neuroscience & therapeutics · 2026 Nov
作者：Han Y, Xu J, Shen Y, Zhang J et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：综述
摘要：INTRODUCTION: Epilepsy is a complex neurological disorder characterized by recurrent seizures. A substantial proportion of patients develop drug-resistant epilepsy (DRE), highlighting the limitations of current therapies and the need to view epilepsy beyond the traditional "channelopathy" hypothesis as a multifactorial network disorder. METHOD: This narrative review synthesizes current evidence on epilepsy pathogenesis, mechanisms of drug resistance, and emerging therapeutic strategies. Relevant studies were identified through literature searches of PubMed and Web of Science, with emphasis on publications from 2018 to 2025 and landmark studies that have shaped current understanding of epileptogenesis, drug-resistant epilepsy, and precision therapy. RESULTS: The pathogenesis of epilepsy converges genetic, neuroinflammatory, and cellular homeostasis pathways. Key drivers include ion channel mutations, glia-mediated neuroinflammation, and dysregulated processes like autophagy and ferroptosis. These contribute to drug resistance and comorbidities, driving a therapeutic shift toward precision and network-modulation strategies, such as gene therapy, targeted pathway modulation, and neuromodulation, with the ultimate goal of achieving disease modification rather than seizure suppression alone. CONCLUSIONS: Integrating multi-omics insights to construct a holistic precision medicine framework, spanning from molecular diagnosis to tailored network therapy, is fundamental for advancing epilepsy treatment. Future efforts must focus on translating this integrated understanding into effective clinical strategies to overcome therapeutic resistance.
Proton Pump Inhibitor Use and Incident Epilepsy in the UK Biobank: Evidence From Pharmacovigilance and Multi-Omics Analyses.推荐
PMID 42980094 · CNS neuroscience & therapeutics · 2026 Nov
作者：Li M, Li Y, Zhang Y, Saibou BS et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：AIMS: Proton pump inhibitors (PPIs) are widely used, but their association with incident epilepsy remains uncertain. We examined this association in the UK Biobank and integrated evidence from pharmacovigilance and multi-omics. METHODS: We included 492,858 UK Biobank participants without epilepsy at baseline. Cox models, age- and sex-stratified analyses, active-comparator analyses against H2RA users, propensity score matching, competing-risk models, and sensitivity analyses were performed. FAERS disproportionality, Olink proteomics, and NMR metabolomics provided supportive evidence. RESULTS: Baseline PPI use was associated with incident epilepsy after full adjustment (HR 1.43, 95% CI 1.29-1.59) and in the PPI-H2RA propensity score-matched analysis (HR 1.41, 95% CI 1.03-1.94). In the GP-linked subset, requiring an ASM prescription within 365 days after diagnosis yielded HR 1.47 (95% CI 1.07-2.02). FAERS showed a PPI-epilepsy reporting signal (ROR 1.70, 95% CI 1.61-1.79). Proteomic and metabolomic analyses identified immune-inflammatory and metabolic associations. CONCLUSION: Baseline PPI use was associated with incident epilepsy in the UK Biobank. Active-comparator, outcome-definition, and prescription-supported analyses supported the primary association. Pharmacovigilance and molecular findings provided complementary evidence but did not establish causality.
In Silico Evaluation of the Effects of Temperature on the Affinity of the SV2C Ligand UCB-1A to SV2 Paralogs.浏览
PMID 42980084 · ACS chemical neuroscience · 2026 Nov 04
作者：Zou R, Nag S, Sousa V, Morén A et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：Synaptic vesicle glycoproteins 2 (SV2) are integral membrane proteins essential for neurotransmitter release and are implicated in neurological disorders including epilepsy and Parkinson's disease. In the attempt to develop a ligand selective for SV2C, and i in collaboration with UCB Biopharma, UCB-F was identified as a potential candidate. However, the affinity of UCB-F to SV2C was found to be temperature dependent, decreasing by about 10-fold from 4 to 37 °C. UCB-1A was subsequently identified as SV2C ligand displaying in vitro a 100-fold selectivity for SV2C compared with SV2A. In this study, we investigated whether the binding of UCB-1A to SV2A and SV2C was affected by the temperature. A combination of experimental binding assay data and molecular dynamics (MD) simulations were used. The binding studies revealed that UCB-1A affinity for SV2A decreased significantly at 37 °C compared with 4 °C, whereas binding to SV2C remained largely unchanged. MD simulations reproduced these observations, namely that ligand RMSD values at 37 °C showed that UCB-1A binding fluctuated markedly in the SV2A complex, with many trajectories exceeding the 3.0 Å stability cutoff, whereas UCB-1A remained relatively well-anchored in SV2C under the same conditions. Structural analysis showed that, while UCB-1A adopts a conserved binding pose across all paralogs stabilized by π-π stacking and a hydrogen bond with Asp, SV2C possesses a unique stabilizing feature. In SV2C, Tyr298 is less exposed to the solvent and engages in a persistent hydrogen bond with Asparagine, a structural feature that reinforces pocket stability and limits temperature-induced destabilization. This interaction is absent in SV2A, consistent with its greater temperature sensitivity. Together, these findings provide a mechanistic explanation for the experimentally observed temperature independence of UCB-1A binding to SV2C. More broadly, the results highlight the importance of incorporating physiologically relevant temperatures into SV2 ligand evaluation and demonstrate how combining experiments with simulations can uncover isoform-specific mechanisms of ligand recognition and stability.

Evaluation of the Consistency of Peri-ictal Hemodynamic Findings Revealed by Capturing More Than 3 Seizures Using 1.5-Tesla Pseudo-continuous Arterial Spin Labeling Magnetic Resonance Perfusion Imaging with Triple Post-labeling Delays: Report of 3 Cases.推荐
PMID 42979421 · NMC case report journal · 2026 10 13
作者：Morioka T, Shimogawa T, Mukae N, Mugita F et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：病例报告
摘要：In the pathophysiological diagnosis of seizures in neuro-emergencies, the usefulness of arterial spin labeling perfusion imaging, which can be performed 24 hr a day, 7 days a week, in capturing peri-ictal hyperperfusion has been reported as a means of compensating for the limitations of electroencephalography, which is available only during regular hours. Herein, we retrospectively evaluated the consistency of arterial spin labeling findings in conjunction with electroencephalography findings in 3 patients with 3 or more seizures. Patients 1 and 2 both experienced 3 seizures and were transported outside of regular hours. On arterial spin labeling, consistent peri-ictal hyperperfusion was observed in the right posterior quadrant with a close anatomical relationship to the epileptogenic lesion in Patient 1 and in the right occipitotemporal lobe without an identifiable epileptogenic lesion in Patient 2. Subsequent electroencephalography failed to reveal any paroxysmal discharges. Patient 3 experienced 4 seizures. Electroencephalography performed immediately after the third seizure demonstrated ictal discharges in the right frontal region, while the other electroencephalography recordings showed paroxysmal discharges and rhythmic slow waves in the same region. In arterial spin labeling, hyperperfusion was observed in the right frontal lobe only during the second seizure, whereas hypoperfusion was observed in the same region during the other seizures. This pattern was likely attributable to an early transition from ictal hyperperfusion to postictal or interictal hypoperfusion, and was therefore considered a consistent arterial spin labeling finding. Although consistent peri-ictal hyperperfusion is commonly observed on arterial spin labeling, hypoperfusion may also be observed; as such, comprehensive evaluation, including electroencephalography findings, is required for accurate diagnosis.
Three-Dimensional Gait Analysis in STXBP1-Related Disorder.推荐
PMID 42978982 · Neurology. Genetics · 2026 11 02
作者：Swinnen M, Gys L, Thalwitzer KM, Deporte A et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：BACKGROUND AND OBJECTIVES: STXBP1-related disorder (STXBP1-RD), caused by pathogenic variants in the STXBP1 gene, is a rare neurodevelopmental condition, characterized by early-onset seizures, developmental delay, intellectual disability (ID), and prominent motor dysfunction. Despite the high prevalence of motor symptoms, systematic gait characterization remains limited. The aim of this study was therefore to quantitatively assess gait in participants with STXBP1-RD. METHODS: In this cross-sectional study, we included ambulatory participants aged 6 years or older with genetically confirmed STXBP1-RD. Instrumented 3D gait analysis (i3DGA) was performed to objectively quantify gait. Functional mobility was assessed with the Functional Mobility Scale (FMS) and Mobility Questionnaire 28 (MobQues28). Caregiver health-related quality of life was evaluated using the PedsQL-Family Impact Module (PedsQL-FIM). We explored associations between gait, functional mobility, STXBP1-variant type, and clinical features (ID, age at seizure onset, seizure frequency, and age at onset of independent walking). Correspondence between i3DGA and the Edinburgh Visual Gait Score (EVGS), an observational gait assessment, was investigated. RESULTS: Nineteen participants were included. Compared with typically developing peers, participants with STXBP1-RD had significantly reduced walking speed, step length, and stride length. Gait patterns were highly variable, with the most frequent pattern being an externally rotated foot progression angle (FPA), present in 15 of 19 participants. At home, 94.12% of the participants (16/17) walked independently, yet community mobility was more variable: 70.59% (12/17) walked independently, 11.76% (2/17) with aid, and 17.65% (3/17) used a wheelchair, indicating increasing limitations with distance and environmental complexity. Earlier acquisition of independent walking strongly predicted later unassisted ambulation at the community level (p < 0.001). The median MobQues28 score was 57.14% and median PedsQL-FIM score was 60.42%, indicating a moderate level of mobility limitations and reduced health-related quality of life of caregivers. EVGS was highly positive correlated with i3DGA (p = 0.001). DISCUSSION: Quantitative gait analysis in participants with STXBP1-RD demonstrates heterogenous kinematic deviations, with an externally rotated FPA emerging as the most common pattern. Age at independent walking was a clinically relevant predictor of later functional mobility. EVGS showed strong correspondence with i3DGA and may offer a more practical, semiquantitative assessment for broader use. These findings inform clinical decision making and guide the selection of scalable outcome measures for natural history studies and interventional trials.

A network-control approach to seizure intervention via adjoint optimal control: a computational study on a connectome-coupled Hopf oscillator model.推荐
PMID 42978792 · Cognitive neurodynamics · 2026 11 02
作者：Muralidhara S, Deosthale A, Anil Y
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：Most existing open-loop Deep Brain Stimulation (DBS) systems for drug-resistant epilepsy do not take into account the temporal dynamics of seizures or the spread of seizure activity. In this paper, we propose a closed-loop control system that models seizure prevention as a constraint on the nonlinear dynamics of the brain's structural network, and we evaluate it entirely in simulation. The structural network of the brain is modeled using the HCP-1065 (Human Connectome Project) connectome, which divides the brain into 360 regions. A densely connected sub-network of 60 brain regions was used to develop and test the proposed control system. Each region is modeled as a nonlinear supercritical Hopf oscillator, and the seizures modeled are focal-onset events that spread from a hippocampal onset zone through the connectome. A detector continuously monitors critical-slowing-down features of the dynamics, such as variance and lag-1 autocorrelation, to identify the approach to seizure activity. Two levels of control are applied: a linear-quadratic regulator (Level 1) that maintains the Hopf oscillators' basal state, and a dormant network-wide optimal controller (Level 2) that employs an adjoint-based optimal-control method (Adjoint Optimal Control; AOC) to provide pre-emptive control. Within the model, the predictive detector identifies the approach to the seizure transition with an area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUC) of 0.889 (95% confidence interval (CI) [0.820, 0.950]) on an independent test fold containing 50 trajectories per class, with a mean lead time of 56.9 model-time seconds; at the recall-maximizing operating point its false-positive rate is high (0.90 per 120-s seizure-free record), which we identify as the principal limitation of the present design. The controller prevented the seizure-like transition in 93% of 100 Monte Carlo simulations (95% CI: 86.2--96.6%) on the 60-node sub-network, against 21% for a connectome-naive targeting baseline; in the same continuously seizure-prone benchmark, a continuous open-loop push at matched amplitude and a PI controller suppressed every trial at lower energy, so the closed loop's advantages lie in pre-emptive lead time, network-wide homeostasis and delivering no stimulation when no risk is detected, not in energy per event. All validation uses synthetic trajectories generated by the same model; no patient or EEG data are used, and the results constitute a methodological proof-of-concept rather than evidence of clinical efficacy.
Case Report: A novel deep intronic MFSD8 splice-altering variant causes CLN7 disease through pseudoexon inclusion.浏览
PMID 42978612 · Frontiers in genetics · 2026 10 20
作者：Akimova D, Voskanyan A, Orlova A, Skoblov M
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：病例报告
摘要：Pathogenic deep intronic variants are an underrecognized cause of inherited disorders because they are typically missed by exon-focused sequencing and often require functional validation. We describe a patient with classic CLN7 disease caused by a previously unreported deep intronic variant in MFSD8. The patient presented with developmental regression, progressive epilepsy, cerebellar ataxia, and cerebral and cerebellar atrophy. Trio whole-genome sequencing identified a homozygous MFSD8 variant, NM_001371596.2:c.999-556C>G, absent from population databases and previous reports. SpliceAI predicted activation of cryptic splice sites leading to inclusion of a 98-nt pseudoexon. RNA analysis of patient-derived fibroblasts confirmed aberrant pseudoexon inclusion, resulting in a frameshift and premature termination codon, with the aberrant transcript accounting for 84% of total MFSD8 transcripts. A minigene assay independently reproduced the splicing defect, confirming that the variant disrupts normal MFSD8 splicing. This study expands the mutational spectrum of MFSD8 and provides functional evidence supporting the pathogenicity of a novel deep intronic splice-altering variant. Our findings further demonstrate the value of combining whole-genome sequencing with transcript analysis for the molecular diagnosis of unsolved rare neurogenetic disorders.
Investigating cardiorespiratory fitness, cerebral blood flow, and brain metabolism in epilepsy.推荐
PMID 42978561 · Frontiers in network physiology · 2026 10 20
作者：Lien Y, Mitchell BS, Szaflarski JP, Ver Hoef L et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：INTRODUCTION: Idiopathic generalized epilepsies (IGEs) are characterized by seizures that propagate through widespread thalamocortical networks including the anterior cingulate cortex (ACC). Poor cardiorespiratory fitness may exacerbate neurovascular and metabolic vulnerability including heightened susceptibility to sudden unexpected death in epilepsy. We aimed to examine cardiorespiratory fitness, regional cerebral blood flow (CBF), and metabolite concentrations in patients with IGEs (PwIGEs) and to determine the effect of seizure control status on these physiological and neurochemical measures. METHODS: Forty-six participants (31 PwIGEs, 15 healthy controls) underwent a graded exercise test to assess maximal oxygen uptake (VO2peak) as a measure of cardiorespiratory fitness and completed 3T MRI including multi-delayed pseudo-continuous arterial spin labeling (pCASL) to assess CBF and single-voxel spectroscopy (SVS) to assess ratios of N-acetylaspartate and glutamate/glutamine complex to total creatine (NAA/tCr and Glx/tCr, respectively) in the ACC and thalamus. Group differences and seizure control effects in PwIGEs were examined using analysis of covariance (ANCOVA). RESULTS: VO2peak, thalamic CBF, ACC NAA/tCr, and ACC Glx/tCr did not significantly differ between groups. PwIGEs exhibited higher ACC CBF (p = 0.039) and lower thalamic NAA/tCr (p = 0.012) than healthy controls. PwIGEs with active seizures showed significantly lower thalamic Glx/tCr compared to those who were seizure-free (p = 0.003). DISCUSSION: These findings suggest poor cardiorespiratory fitness is not epilepsy-specific and likely attributable to lifestyle and that region-specific alterations in perfusion and metabolic milieu in PwIGEs characterized by higher ACC CBF and lower thalamic NAA/tCr and Glx/tCr in those with active seizures. The multimodal neuroimaging approach provides convergent evidence of thalamocortical network dysregulation underlying IGE pathophysiology.
Expert Review on the Use of Methylphenidate in Children and Adult With Epilepsy and Attention Deficit Hyperactivity Disorder.推荐
PMID 42978542 · Revista de neurologia · 2026 Oct 29
作者：Soto Insuga V, Russi ME, González Alguacil E, López Pisón J et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：综述
摘要：Attention deficit hyperactivity disorder (ADHD) is a comorbidity affecting 12%-29% of individuals with epilepsy, significantly impacting learning, psychosocial development, behaviour, and quality of life. Moreover, ADHD and epilepsy share a bidirectional relationship. Although methylphenidate has been proven effective in alleviating ADHD symptoms, its use remains limited in individuals with epilepsy due to outdated concerns-dating back over 20 years-that psychostimulants could increase seizure frequency. This manuscript presents a narrative review of the scientific literature published between January 2000 and May 2026 regarding the use of methylphenidate in patients with epilepsy and ADHD. Based on the available evidence, a panel of paediatric neurologists from five Spanish tertiary hospitals with expertise in epilepsy and neurodevelopmental disorders reached an expert consensus and developed a series of practical recommendations for the assessment and management of these patients. This expert group issued several recommendations, including the proactive screening of ADHD symptoms in all children and adults with epilepsy from the onset of the condition. Current evidence has not consistently demonstrated an increased seizure risk associated with methylphenidate in patients with epilepsy, although available data remain limited for some epilepsy subgroups. Furthermore, undertreatment of ADHD is associated with poorer patient outcomes. Methylphenidate may be considered in children and adults with epilepsy when clinically indicated, following optimization of epilepsy management, associated comorbidities, and behavioural and psychosocial interventions. Treatment decisions should be individualized, particularly in patients with active epilepsy, drug-resistant epilepsy, recent seizure worsening, or developmental and epileptic encephalopathies.
PIK3R2-related megalencephaly syndrome: expanded phenotypic spectrum associated with craniosynostosis.推荐
PMID 42978020 · Orphanet journal of rare diseases · 2026 10 22
作者：Digkas E, Körberg IB, Mourtos E, Nowinski D et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：病例报告
摘要：BACKGROUND: Megalencephaly-polymicrogyria-polydactyly-hydrocephalus (MPPH) syndrome is a rare neurodevelopmental disorder caused by activating variants in the PI3K-AKT-mTOR signaling pathway, most frequently in PIK3R2. The recurrent variant c.1117G > A (p.Gly373Arg) has been associated with classical MPPH features including megalencephaly, bilateral polymicrogyria, hydrocephalus, and polydactyly. However, the phenotypic spectrum is still expanding. METHODS: We describe a 7-year-old girl with developmental delay, epilepsy, and structural brain anomalies. Trio-based whole exome sequencing followed by Sanger validation was performed. Clinical and radiological evaluations were conducted, including brain MRI, CT, and ophthalmological assessment. RESULTS: The patient exhibited congenital megalencephaly, bilateral perisylvian and parietotemporal polymicrogyria, and hypotonia. She lacked polydactyly but presented with complex bicoronal and sagittal craniosynostosis, representing a potential novel association with this variant. Imaging revealed signs of chronic intracranial hypertension, including optic nerve tortuosity, papilledema, pituitary flattening, and gyral impressions. A heterozygous de novo PIK3R2 c.1117G > A (p.Gly373Arg) variant was identified and classified as pathogenic. CONCLUSION: This report broadens the phenotypic spectrum of PIK3R2-related MPPH syndrome by describing a potential novel association with craniosynostosis, accompanied by radiologic signs of intracranial hypertension. The findings support the inclusion of PIK3R2 in genetic work-ups for patients with cortical malformations, megalencephaly, and syndromic presentations-even in the absence of polydactyly.
Epilepsy: epidemiology, risk factors, classification, pathophysiology, clinical features, diagnosis and management.推荐
PMID 42977736 · Signal transduction and targeted therapy · 2026 11 04
作者：Sha L, Fu Y, Basang S, Jin L et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：综述
摘要：Epilepsy is one of the most prevalent and disabling neurological disorders, affecting >50 million people worldwide. Beyond recurrent seizures, it is strongly associated with cognitive impairment, psychiatric comorbidities, reduced social participation, and a markedly increased risk of premature death. Although considerable advances in neuroimaging, pharmacology, and surgical techniques have expanded therapeutic possibilities, nearly one-third of patients remain refractory to current treatments, a challenge that highlights the pressing need for deeper mechanistic insights and innovative therapeutic strategies. This review provides an integrated overview of recent progress in epilepsy research and treatment. At the molecular level, we examine how ion channel dysfunction, receptor abnormalities, and disrupted signaling pathways drive hyperexcitability. We highlight insights from genetics and acquired etiologies that illuminate disease heterogeneity. We address prevailing hypotheses of drug resistance and potential biomarkers. We also emphasize the growing recognition of epilepsy's close association with diverse comorbidities, where shared molecular pathways not only deepen our understanding of disease mechanisms but also highlight new therapeutic targets. Finally, we evaluate emerging therapeutic directions, including precision pharmacology, neuromodulation, gene and cell therapies, and metabolic approaches such as the ketogenic diet. By bridging epidemiological perspectives with mechanistic discoveries and innovative therapies, this review underscores how advances in epilepsy research are reshaping the path toward precision medicine and improved patient outcomes.
Intron Retention Caused by a Novel Homozygous LNPK Splice-Site Variant Leading to Severe Neurodevelopmental Disorder and Epilepsy.推荐
PMID 42977535 · American journal of medical genetics. Part B, Neuropsychiatric genetics : the official publication of the International Society of Psychiatric Genetics · 2026 11 03
作者：Gökpınar İli E, İlbasmış H, Altıner Ş, Kaynak Şahap S et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：Loss-of-function variants in LNPK have been shown to cause a neurodevelopmental syndrome associated with developmental delay, epilepsy, regression, and a distinct neuroimaging pattern including corpus callosum hypoplasia and abnormal T2/FLAIR hyperintensity at the tip of the frontal horn of the lateral ventricles ("ear-of-the-lynx" sign). To date, 21 affected individuals from 16 families have been reported. Here, we identify a novel homozygous LNPK splice-site variant (NM_030650.3:c.27 + 1G>T) in a patient with developmental delay, severe intellectual disability, epilepsy, and a brain MRI demonstrating corpus callosum hypoplasia, "ear-of-the-lynx" sign, and substantia nigra signal alterations. To determine the variant's pathogenicity, we first used in silico splice prediction models AlphaGenome, SAI-10 k-calc, SpliceAI, and Pangolin. Then, by implementing targeted RNA sequencing of patient-derived fibroblasts following nonsense-mediated decay inhibition, we showed that the variant causes activation of a cryptic donor site, which results in a 5 bp intron retention and introduction of a premature stop codon into the transcript. We also delineate the phenotypic features of the proband with a comprehensive review of the literature. These findings expand the molecular spectrum of LNPK deficiency and demonstrate the complementary value of computational splicing prediction and functional RNA analysis for establishing the consequences of splice-site variants in rare-disease diagnostics.
Changes in the profile of adults diagnosed as autistic since 2010: population-based studies in the UK and Sweden.推荐
PMID 42977525 · BMJ mental health · 2026 11 03
作者：Sadik A, Lundberg M, Khandaker GM, Pardiñas AF et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：BACKGROUND: Autism diagnoses have risen consistently over time. Characterising the backgrounds of adults being diagnosed with autism can help to clarify why. OBJECTIVE: To understand if sociodemographic and neuropsychiatric characteristics of adults diagnosed with autism in the UK and Sweden have changed since 2010. METHODS: Using UK primary care records from 2010 to 2023 and Swedish population-wide register linkages from 2010 to 2021, we calculated annual age-standardised incidence and prevalence of adult autism diagnoses within different sociodemographic groups. We also calculated annual age-standardised proportion of adults with prior records of other neuropsychiatric conditions or medications by autism diagnosis status. FINDINGS: In the UK, 24 537 039 adults contributed to the study, including 141 119 with an autism diagnosis. In Sweden, 9 096 874 adults contributed, including 100 817 with an autism diagnosis. Incident (first recorded) adult autism diagnoses were consistently higher in Sweden than the UK; however, incidence increased rapidly in the UK after 2020. Incident diagnoses increased fastest for 16–20 year-olds and females in both nations, as well as people in white ethnic groups in the UK and people with Swedish-born parents in Sweden. Over time, there has been a consistent decline in the proportion of autistic adults with a prior diagnosis of epilepsy, psychosis and intellectual disability and an increase in the proportion with a prior diagnosis of attention-deficit hyperactivity disorder, anxiety, depression and several other mental illnesses. For example, in the UK between 2010 and 2023, a history of epilepsy among adults with newly recorded autism decreased from 10% (95% CI 7.6% to 13%) to 4% (95% CI 3.6% to 4.5%), while a history of anxiety increased from 28.7% (95% CI 24.4% to 33.6%) to 58.3% (95% CI 56.6% to 60.1%). Trends were broadly consistent between females and males. CONCLUSIONS: The characteristics of individuals diagnosed as autistic have changed dramatically since 2010 in both the UK and Sweden. CLINICAL IMPLICATIONS: Service design and clinical practice around autistic healthcare should account for inequitable distribution of autism diagnoses in society, the diagnostic complexity associated with other mental health conditions and apparent changes in the average health needs of autistic groups. The extent to which these changes indicate nuanced recognition of autism or broadening of diagnostic practice needs investigation.
Chronotherapy and Circadian Medicine: Personalizing Treatment Timing with Artificial Intelligence.推荐
PMID 42977364 · Sleep medicine clinics · 2026 09 28
作者：Bahammam SA
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：综述
摘要：This review examines how artificial intelligence (AI) is transforming chronotherapy and circadian medicine by enabling precise, individualized estimation of circadian phase. AI algorithms applied to wearable sensors, blood transcriptomes, and multimodal physiologic data now predict internal body times, supporting optimized timing of light therapy, chemotherapy, antiseizure medications, and antihypertensives. While promising applications exist in sleep disorders, oncology, epilepsy, and cardiometabolic conditions, significant barriers remain in data standardization, regulatory approval, and clinical integration. The article highlights recent advances, critically reviews controversial trials (eg, Hygia), and outlines practical steps toward translating AI-guided chronotherapy into routine sleep and medical practice.
When to use first-generation anti-obesity medications: Mechanisms, clinical indications, and patient-focused selection.浏览
PMID 42977348 · Advances in pharmacology (San Diego, Calif.) · 2026 10 26
作者：Burguera B, Griebeler ML, Al-Husni D
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：综述
摘要：UNLABELLED: The development of high efficacy, second-generation incretin-based anti-obesity medications (AOMs) has changed the landscape of pharmacotherapy of obesity. However, first-generation AOMS, such as orlistat, as well as the combination of phentermine/topiramate extended release (ER) and naltrexone sustained release (SR)/bupropion SR and liraglutide 3.0 mg retain an important and evidence-based role in clinical practice, still producing more than 5 % but usually less than 10 % placebo-subtracted total body weight loss on average. OBJECTIVE: Building on the complication-integrated diagnostic framework proposed in Chapter 2, this chapter presents a systematic approach for the selection, initiation and monitoring of first-generation AOMs. The four variables that influence the selection of an agent are: magnitude of weight reduction required to achieve comorbidity-specific benefit, patient's phenotypic and complication profile, safety and tolerability, and real-world access to these medications and cost considerations. KEY FINDINGS: The choice of anti-obesity medications should be individualized and influenced not only by expected weight-loss efficacy but also by obesity-related complications, medication access, cost, prior treatment response and tolerability, and most importantly, patient preferences. For example, liraglutide and even phentermine/topiramate ER may be particularly attractive in those patients with prediabetes or at risk for type 2 diabetes, while naltrexone/Bupropion SR may offer added benefits in patients with history of depression, emotional eating, or concurrent use of tobacco or alcohol use. Phentermine/Topiramate ER may also have a dual therapeutic effect in people with migraine, epilepsy, and food cravings. There is also evidence of the benefits of using these medications in individuals experiencing recurrent weight gain after metabolic surgery. Bayesian network meta-analyses consistently show a hierarchy of efficacy for weight loss, with tirzepatide and semaglutide resulting in the greatest average weight loss. However, first-generation anti-obesity drugs remain significant treatment alternatives when newer agents are contraindicated, poorly tolerated, or inaccessible because of cost or insurance coverage. CONCLUSION: First-generation AOMs remain evidence-based, cost-effective components of comprehensive obesity care. Therapeutic selection should be individualized taking into consideration obesity-related complications, patient characteristics, treatment goals, access, tolerability, and patients' preferences rather than only expected weight-loss magnitude.
Seizure and motor outcomes following subtotal hemispherotomy: A retrospective case series and systematic review.推荐
PMID 42977257 · World neurosurgery · 2026 11 03
作者：Krouma M, Hayashi T, Iijima K, Kimura Y et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：PURPOSE: Subtotal hemispherotomy (SH) is a motor-sparing approach for multifocal or hemispheric epilepsy, but its efficacy and optimal indications remain unclear. We evaluated seizure and motor outcomes after SH and the factors associated with seizure freedom. METHODS: We retrospectively analyzed patients who underwent SH at our institution between 2015 and 2024 and conducted a systematic review of studies reporting seizure and motor outcomes after SH. The primary outcome was seizure freedom (International League Against Epilepsy [ILAE] class 1) at last follow-up; seizure-free and non-seizure-free patients were compared. RESULTS: Six institutional patients and 30 patients from the literature were included. In our cohort, seizure freedom (ILAE class 1) was achieved in 3 of 6 patients (50%) at 1 year and in 2 of 6 (33%) at last follow-up (median, 20 months), with no permanent new motor deficits. In the pooled literature cohort, 23 of 30 patients (77%; 95% CI, 58-90) were seizure-free at a median follow-up of 63.5 months; 19 of these patients came from a single, predominantly resective series, and the seizure-freedom rate after disconnective SH was 73% (8/11). No permanent motor deficits were reported. No factor was significantly associated with seizure freedom. CONCLUSION: SH preserved motor function in all patients. However, fewer than half of our patients remained seizure-free at long-term follow-up, a lower rate than reported in the literature. SH may be considered when preservation of motor function is a priority, provided that families are counseled that seizure outcomes may be inferior to those of complete hemispherotomy.
Central nervous system involvement, sequelae, and prognostic markers in children with severe hand-foot-and-mouth disease: A multicenter cohort study across four Chinese cities.推荐
PMID 42977160 · Virologica Sinica · 2026 11 03
作者：Chen Y, Li Y, Wang X, Sun N et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：Approximately 10% of severe hand-foot-mouth disease (HFMD) cases develop post-recovery sequelae such as language and cognitive impairments, posing major social and family burdens. However, large-scale evidence on long-term outcomes is limited. This multicenter cohort study retrospectively analyzed hospitalized HFMD cases between January 1, 2015, and December 31, 2023, in four cities (Zhengzhou, Changchun, Xi'an, and Zhoukou). Among 25,536 medical records, all severe HFMD inpatients were identified, and mild HFMD inpatients from the same period were randomly selected as controls at a 1:1 ratio, resulting in 1,909 patients in each group. Sequelae were assessed through long-term follow-up, and potential prognostic markers were examined using Kaplan-Meier survival analysis and multivariable Cox proportional hazards models. The result showed that severe HFMD was associated with a significantly higher risk of long-term sequelae compared with mild cases, including motor disorders, epilepsy, attention-deficit/hyperactivity disorder, tic disorders, language disorders, developmental delays, psychiatric disorders, intellectual disabilities, and social impairments. Independent predictors of sequelae were peak fever, feeding mode, serum creatinine, serum sodium, and complement C3 levels, which together provided effective prediction of the 6-year cumulative risk. Abnormal magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) and electroencephalography (EEG) findings during the acute phase were strongly correlated with subsequent sequelae. Furthermore, learning disabilities were more common among patients with severe HFMD, further highlighting the potential long-term neurological and neurodevelopmental burden of the disease. Together, these findings underscore the importance of long-term follow-up and further research to improve early identification of children at risk and inform strategies to reduce the long-term effect of HFMD.

PMID 42976724 · Multiple sclerosis and related disorders · 2026 10 21

摘要：OBJECTIVES: Cerebral cortical encephalitis (CCE) is a recognized clinical phenotype of myelin oligodendrocyte glycoprotein antibody-associated disease (MOGAD), for which data regarding long-term disease course and optimal treatment strategies remain limited. MATERIALS AND METHODS: In this multicenter observational study, patients with symptomatic cerebral cortical lesions fulfilling the 2023 MOGAD diagnostic criteria were included. Demographic characteristics, clinical presentation, radiological findings, laboratory results, treatment responses, and long-term outcomes were retrospectively collected and analyzed. RESULTS: Twenty-four patients with anti-MOG-associated CCE were included. Median age at disease onset was 23 (IQR, 17) years, and 54.2% were male. Seizures occurred in 79.2% of patients, including status epilepticus in 31.6%. During follow-up, 62.5% experienced at least one relapse. Cortical lesions were predominantly unilateral (79.2%) and most frequently involved the frontal and parietal lobes, while spinal cord involvement was observed in 33.3% of cases. CONCLUSION: Cerebral cortical encephalitis is a recognized clinical phenotype within the MOGAD spectrum, characterized predominantly by seizures and cortical MRI abnormalities. Although neurological recovery is generally favorable, relapses may occur during long-term follow-up, highlighting the importance of continued clinical surveillance.

Temporal trends in GBD idiopathic epilepsy burden across 43 European countries, 1990-2023.推荐
PMID 42976532 · Seizure · 2026 10 27
作者：Brigo F, Brigiari G, Lattanzi S, Zelano J et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：AIM: To quantify long-term trends in Global Burden of Disease (GBD) idiopathic epilepsy (genetic/unknown-cause epilepsy) across European countries from 1990-2023, examining overall burden, disability, and burden from premature mortality. METHODS: We analyzed age-standardized Disability-Adjusted Life Years (DALY), Years of Life Lost (YLL), and Years Lived with Disability (YLD) in 43 European countries using GBD 2023 data. Country-specific Estimated Annual Percentage Changes (EAPCs) summarized average long-term trends from 1990 to 2023, and longitudinal models estimated the average European trend. Additional analyses tested whether the main findings were sensitive to the shape of the time trend, correlation between nearby years, or the influence of individual and small countries. RESULTS: The burden declined on average across Europe, but national trajectories differed substantially. The estimated average annual changes were -0.59% for DALY (95% CI -0.89 to -0.28), -0.60% for YLL (-1.08 to -0.13), and -0.54% for YLD (-0.73 to -0.35). Some of the largest reductions occurred in selected Eastern European and former Soviet countries, particularly for YLL, but this pattern was not uniform across Eastern Europe. Declines were greater in males than females for all three burden measures. The overall European decline remained consistent across the additional analyses. CONCLUSIONS: Age-standardized DALY, YLL, and YLD rates for GBD idiopathic epilepsy declined on average across 43 European countries from 1990 to 2023. This overall improvement masked major national differences, particularly in burden from premature mortality. Country-specific monitoring remains important because the European average does not describe all national trajectories.
Predicting the risk of seizure recurrence after antiseizure medication withdrawal in children with epilepsy: An individualized and web-based clinical decision support model.推荐

命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：BACKGROUND: To determine seizure recurrence rates and independent risk factors following antiseizure medication (ASM) withdrawal in pediatric epilepsy, and integrate these findings into a personalized web-based clinical decision support system (CDSS). METHODS: In this prospective cohort study, 318 pediatric epilepsy patients with ≥24 months of clinical seizure freedom and completely normal pre-withdrawal EEGs were evaluated during ASM withdrawal. Independent risk factors were identified using a multivariable Cox proportional hazards model and embedded into a dynamic web tool. RESULTS: Seizure recurrence occurred in 25.8% (n = 82) of patients (mean time: 4.70 ± 13.96 months). The Cox model demonstrated acceptable discrimination (C-index = 0.808, optimism-corrected C-index = 0.769). Independent predictors of recurrence were status epilepticus (SE; HR = 2.718, 95% CI: 1.193-6.194; p = 0.017), febrile seizures (FS; HR = 2.300, 95% CI: 1.214-4.356; p = 0.011), and active epilepsy duration (HR = 1.022 per month, 95% CI: 1.016-1.027; p < 0.001). Compared to ≤24 months, pre-withdrawal seizure freedom of 25-35 months (HR = 0.231, p = 0.001) and ≥36 months (HR = 0.232, p = 0.002) both conferred an approximate 77% risk reduction. Mandatory pre-withdrawal EEG normalization served as a controlled methodological feature isolating clinical predictors in EEG-normal children. Among 35 patients undergoing a second withdrawal attempt, 11 (31.4%) experienced recurrence. CONCLUSIONS: SE, FS, active epilepsy duration, and pre-withdrawal seizure-free duration independently predict recurrence in children with normal pre-withdrawal EEGs. Embedding this model into a web tool enables dynamic, evidence-based risk stratification in pediatric practice.
Full title: From neural excitability to volatile signatures: A mechanistic hypothesis for pre-ictal volatile organic compound signatures in epilepsy.推荐
PMID 42976516 · Epilepsy research · 2026 10 28
作者：Bearden DJ, Galanti AM, Rehim E, Wheless JW et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：综述
摘要：OBJECTIVE: Seizure unpredictability remains a major contributor to morbidity in epilepsy, and biomarkers capable of identifying pre-ictal states are critically needed. Volatile organic compounds (VOCs) have emerged as potential noninvasive indicators of seizure susceptibility, yet the biological mechanisms linking pre-ictal neural states to peripheral volatile signatures remain poorly understood. METHODS: We conducted a narrative review in accordance with the Scale for the Assessment of Narrative Review Articles (SANRA) criteria integrating evidence from epilepsy neurobiology, oxidative stress, autonomic physiology, volatilomics, and seizure-detection science to develop a mechanistic hypothesis linking pre-ictal physiological processes to seizure-associated volatile signatures. RESULTS: Evidence supports several components of the proposed mechanistic hypothesis, although the strength and directness of evidence vary across pathway components. Pre-ictal states have been associated with increased neuronal excitability, altered neurophysiology, and heightened metabolic demand, creating conditions favorable for oxidative stress. Lipid peroxidation generates volatile and semi-volatile metabolites that can enter systemic circulation and become detectable peripherally. Autonomic modulation may further influence their expression and detectability. Human olfactory and analytical studies provide preliminary evidence for seizure-associated and pre-ictal volatile signatures. Although the complete pathway has not been experimentally validated, available evidence is consistent with a biologically plausible hypothesis linking central physiological processes to peripheral volatile signals. SIGNIFICANCE: This review proposes a multilevel mechanistic hypothesis for seizure-associated volatile signature generation. Volatile signatures are conceptualized as downstream reflections of physiological states associated with seizure susceptibility. This framework provides a foundation for future investigation of VOC-based seizure forecasting approaches in epilepsy.
Real-world outcomes of continuous infusion of sedative agents in critically ill patients with invasive mechanical ventilation.浏览
PMID 42976438 · Journal of critical care · 2026 11 03
作者：Wu CC, Chien HT, Lu SH, Tseng YJ et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：PURPOSE: Current guidelines recommend non-benzodiazepine (non-BZD) sedatives -dexmedetomidine (DEX) and propofol (PPF) - over benzodiazepines (BZD) in mechanically ventilated critically ill patients, but real-world evidence from Asian patients remains limited. We evaluated the clinical outcomes of different continuous-infusion sedatives in this population in Taiwan. MATERIALS AND METHODS: This retrospective cohort study used electronic health records from a tertiary medical center in Taiwan. Adults receiving continuous intravenous sedation within 48 h of intubation were included. Patients with preexisting ventilator dependence, status epilepticus, or prior tracheostomy were excluded. Groups were 1:1 propensity score matched. Outcomes included intensive care unit (ICU) length of stay (LOS), time to extubation, mortality, and total hospitalization cost. Prespecified pairwise comparisons evaluated DEX vs. BZD, PPF vs. BZD, and DEX vs. PPF. RESULTS: Of 2065 eligible patients, 605 matched pairs were analyzed. Compared with BZD, non-BZD sedation was associated with shorter ICU LOS (9.4 vs. 11.9 days, P = 0.032), earlier extubation (4.1 vs. 6.5 days, P = 0.015), and lower hospitalization cost (US$21,559 vs. US$26,258, P < 0.001), without a significant difference in 90-day mortality (36.7% vs. 38.7%, P = 0.489). In pairwise comparisons with BZD, DEX was associated with significantly shorter ICU LOS and time to extubation, whereas the benefit of PPF was primarily a reduction in hospitalization cost. CONCLUSIONS: Non-BZD sedatives were associated with shorter ICU LOS, earlier extubation, and lower hospitalization costs without a detectable mortality difference. These findings are consistent with guideline recommendations favoring non-BZD sedation, although residual confounding remains possible.
Awareness, attitudes toward epilepsy, and first-aid knowledge of seizures among rural primary care doctors in Eastern China.推荐
PMID 42976386 · Journal of clinical neuroscience : official journal of the Neurosurgical Society of Australasia · 2026 11 03
作者：Xu W, Sun M, Wang Y, Xu Z et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：OBJECTIVE: We aimed to assess the awareness of, and attitudes toward epilepsy among young rural primary care doctors from rural clinics in Zhejiang Province, Eastern China. METHODS: Ninety-five young rural primary care doctors working at rural clinics participated in this cross-sectional study in 2025. A custom-built electronic questionnaire, which comprised four sections, including 12 items for general condition data, 10 items for awareness (including first aid), a 16-item scale for attitudes, and 6 items for demographic data, was administered to the rural primary care doctors. Descriptive statistics, the Mann-Whitney U test, the Kruskal-Wallis H test, and Spearman's rank correlation analysis were used to analyze the data, which exhibited a non-normal distribution. Multivariable linear regression analyzes were conducted to identify independent predictors for each outcome, using the enter method and reporting β, B with 95% CI, p-values, and R2. RESULTS: The young rural primary care doctors obtained a mean score of 18.20 ± 3.48 (aggregate score: 30) on the awareness of epilepsy section, a mean score of 5.40 ± 1.53 (aggregate score: 7) on the first aid knowledge section, and a mean score of 66.05 ± 7.65 (aggregate score: 80) on the epilepsy attitude section. Educational background (rs = 0.293, p = 0.004), training experience (rs = 0.322, p = 0.001), and epilepsy attitude scores (rs = 0.255, p = 0.013) correlated significantly with mean epilepsy awareness scores. Training experience (rs = 0.222, p = 0.031) correlated significantly with attitudes toward epilepsy. Multivariable regression analyzes showed that educational background independently predicted epilepsy awareness (p = 0.016) and first aid knowledge (p < 0.001), while training experience did not (p = 0.492 and p = 0.687, respectively). Epilepsy awareness independently predicted attitudes toward people with epilepsy (PWE) (p = 0.004) and first aid knowledge (p < 0.001). Furthermore, when rural primary care doctors faced newly diagnosed PWE, a referral was usually recommended (95.79%), and a few would initiate antiseizure medications (ASMs) (22.58%). In addition, about 30%-40% of the rural primary care doctors maintained an uncertain attitude toward the marriage, dating and pregnancy. CONCLUSION: The awareness and attitudes of young rural primary care doctors toward epilepsy in the rural clinics were suboptimal. We recommend targeted training for young rural primary care doctors (covering first aid, novel ASMs, and patient counseling for marriage, dating, and pregnancy), combined with telemedicine integration to establish specialist support networks and optimize rural epilepsy care pathways.
CLN2 Disease推荐
PMID 42976253 · GeneReviews® · 2026-11-3
作者：De Los Reyes E, Abreu N, Scherr J, Rogers D
链接：PubMed
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：图书章节/更新
摘要：CLINICAL CHARACTERISTICS: CLN2 disease is a progressive lysosomal storage disease with a phenotypic spectrum that ranges from classic CLN2 disease to non-classic CLN2 disease. Classic CLN2 disease: Characterized by language delay followed by generalized- or focal-onset seizures typically between ages two and four years followed by progressive loss of motor and language skills, behavioral manifestations (anxiety, mood lability, hyperactivity, autistic features, aggression, sleep disturbances), and vision loss. Development further declines in early childhood; by age six years, children are often unable to walk or sit unsupported and become blind. Subsequent disease progression is rapid, with a median age of death of ten years. Targeted therapy (cerliponase alfa) can prolong survival. Non-classic CLN2 disease: Characterized by later onset and a more insidious disease course. Median age at onset is six years with common initial manifestations of seizures, followed by speech-language abnormalities and behavioral abnormalities. With disease progression, most individuals have cognitive decline, ataxia, and movement disorders (dystonia/parkinsonism and myoclonus). Vision loss is much less consistently reported in individuals with non-classic CLN2 disease than in individuals with classic CLN2 disease. Some individuals with non-classic CLN2 disease present with ataxia without ophthalmologic findings or epilepsy. The prognosis varies greatly, with some individuals living into early adulthood and others living into their sixties and beyond. DIAGNOSIS/TESTING: The diagnosis of CLN2 disease is established in a proband with suggestive findings and tripeptidyl-peptidase 1 (TPP1) enzyme deficiency in leukocytes (or dried blood spots or fibroblasts), and/or biallelic pathogenic variants in TPP1 identified by molecular genetic testing. MANAGEMENT: Targeted therapy: Enzyme replacement therapy using cerliponase alfa, a recombinant form of human TPP1 enzyme administered via intracerebroventricular infusion has been approved in the United States to slow the loss of ambulation in children and in Europe for all individuals with CLN2 disease. Supportive care: Multidisciplinary care by specialists in child development and cognitive decline, neurobehavioral/psychiatric manifestations, neurology (management of seizures, movement disorders), ophthalmology, low vision services, feeding therapy, orthopedics, physical medicine and rehabilitation, and physical therapy and occupational therapy; ongoing assessment of need for palliative care involvement and/or home nursing. Surveillance: Routinely scheduled evaluations by treating clinicians are recommended to monitor existing manifestations and the individual's response to cerliponase alfa enzyme therapy and supportive care, as well as to identify emergence of any new manifestations. Evaluation of relatives at risk: It is appropriate to offer testing to asymptomatic at-risk sibs so that those with TPP1 enzyme deficiency and/or biallelic TPP1 pathogenic variants can benefit from prompt initiation of treatment. GENETIC COUNSELING: CLN2 disease is inherited in an autosomal recessive manner. The parents of an affected individual are presumed to be heterozygous for a TPP1 pathogenic variant. If both parents are known to be heterozygous for a TPP1 pathogenic variant, each sib of an affected individual has at conception a 25% chance of being affected, a 50% chance of being an asymptomatic carrier, and a 25% chance of being unaffected and not a carrier. Once the TPP1 pathogenic variants have been identified in an affected family member, molecular genetic carrier testing for at-risk relatives and prenatal/preimplantation genetic testing are possible.
Alpha-Methylacyl-CoA Racemase (AMACR) Deficiency Presenting with Epilepsy, Cognitive Decline, and Pigmentary Retinopathy.推荐
PMID 42976102 · Ophthalmic surgery, lasers & imaging retina · 2026 11 06
作者：Adam S, Muid J, Valastro A, De Salvo G
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：A 66-year-old woman with long-standing epilepsy and progressive cognitive decline presented with bilateral vision loss (best-corrected visual acuity 1.22 logMAR right eye, 1.10 logMAR left eye). Electrodiagnostic testing showed severe rod-cone dysfunction. Fundus autofluorescence revealed bilateral multifocal geographic hypoautofluorescent lesions with hyperautofluorescent borders in a wreath-like pericentral distribution. Optical coherence tomography demonstrated bilateral outer retinal atrophy with ellipsoid zone loss and cystoid macular edema in the left eye. Genetic testing identified a pathogenic AMACR variant (c.154T>C; p.Ser52Pro) and a variant of uncertain significance (c.704C>T; p.Ala235Val). Elevated pristanic acid (152.9 μmol/L) confirmed alpha-methylacyl-CoA racemase (AMACR) deficiency, explaining her neurological disease. Macular edema decreased by 50 μm with topical dorzolamide and a phytanic and pristanic acid-restricted diet, and she reported subjective neurological improvement. Retinal imaging may be the first diagnostic clue to this rare but treatable condition. Ophthalmologists should consider AMACR deficiency in unexplained pigmentary retinopathy with neurological disease.
Targeted gene therapy alleviates epilepsy and hyperexcitability in models of SCN2A deficiency.推荐
PMID 42975956 · The Journal of clinical investigation · 2026 11 03
作者：Zhang Z, Zhang J, Chen X, Deming BA et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：Pathogenic SCN2A loss-of-function (LoF) variants can cause severe, refractory epilepsy with limited treatment options. Existing Scn2a-LoF mouse models exhibit few spontaneous seizures, limiting the dynamic range needed to assess therapeutic efficacy. We therefore generated gene-trap Scn2a-deficient mice on the seizure-prone DBA/2J background. These mice experienced hundreds of spontaneous absence seizures per day, characterized by concurrent behavioral arrest and EEG spike-wave discharges (SWDs). Partial restoration of Scn2a expression reduced seizure burden. Electrophysiological analysis revealed impaired action potential waveforms yet increased repetitive firing, alongside coordinated downregulation of voltage-gated potassium channel genes, potentially contributing to hyperexcitability. Given the broad potassium channel downregulation and prior efficacy of KCNA1 gene therapy in refractory seizure models, we selected KV1.1 as a potential therapeutic target and used a drug-controllable adeno-associated virus (AAV) to express human KCNA1. We found that this intervention normalized elevated neuronal firing and reduced spontaneous seizure burden in Scn2a-deficient mice. Extending these findings to a human cell-based platform, AAV-mediated KCNA1 expression also reduced neuronal firing in human cortical organoids carrying a SCN2A-truncating variant identified in patients. Together, these findings provide proof of principle for drug-controllable KCNA1 gene therapy as a promising treatment avenue for epilepsy related to SCN2A LoF variants.
Late electroencephalographic seizure-risk findings and restricted diffusion on magnetic resonance imaging as seizure-burden markers associated with neurological deficits after status epilepticus in a remote or chronic cohort.推荐
PMID 42975879 · Epilepsia · 2026 11 03
作者：Ohno N, Neshige S, Okada R, Abe T et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：OBJECTIVE: To evaluate whether time-dependent electroencephalographic (EEG) seizure risk findings and restricted diffusion on magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) are associated with neurological outcomes after status epilepticus (SE), beyond seizure duration. METHODS: This two-center observational study included consecutive SE patients from 2017 to 2025. We examined three seizure-related markers: seizure duration ≥ 30 min, a 2HELPS2B score ≥ 2 on a late EEG (performed the following day or later after SE onset), and SE-related restricted diffusion on MRI (peri-ictal diffusion-weighted imaging hyperintensity with reduced apparent diffusion coefficient values). Outcomes were overall neurological worsening and SE-attributable focal neurological deficits at discharge. Multivariable models included the three markers and age> 65 years, with further adjustment for stupor or coma at admission and third-line therapy. RESULTS: Of 440 patients with suspected SE, 297 had confirmed SE; 221 with an identified seizure focus formed the overall cohort; 127 with remote symptomatic SE or preexisting chronic focal epilepsy formed the remote or chronic cohort. Overall neurological worsening occurred in 96 patients (43.4%) and SE-attributable focal neurological deficits in 68 (30.8%). In univariable analyses, seizure duration ≥ 30 min was associated with both outcomes in the overall cohort. A 2HELPS2B score ≥ 2 on late EEG was associated with both outcomes in both cohorts, whereas high-risk findings on early EEG were not. In multivariable models including seizure duration, restricted diffusion was independently associated with both outcomes in both cohorts, most strongly with focal neurological deficits in the remote or chronic cohort (adjusted odds ratio [OR] = 28.59, 95% confidence interval [CI] = 7.23-154.99). A 2HELPS2B score ≥ 2 on late EEG was independently associated with focal neurological deficits in the remote or chronic cohort (adjusted OR = 4.26, 95% CI = 1.35-14.24), whereas seizure duration ≥ 30 min was not independently associated with either outcome. SIGNIFICANCE: High-risk late-EEG findings and restricted diffusion were associated with neurological deficits. These markers may complement seizure duration in estimating cumulative seizure burden and focal brain injury.
The risk of seizure-affected pregnancy in Australian women with epilepsy.推荐
PMID 42975788 · Epileptic disorders : international epilepsy journal with videotape · 2026 11 03
作者：Vajda FJE, O'Brien TJ, Graham JE, Hitchcock AE et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：OBJECTIVE: We reported earlier that the occurrence of seizures in the pre-pregnancy year was the main determinant of the following pregnancy being seizure-affected. Additional risk factors were assessed in this study. METHODS: The pregnancies involved first pregnancies of women with epilepsy. Two categories were compared. One category comprised 711 pregnancies in which seizures had occurred during the pre-pregnancy year and where seizures might reasonably be expected to continue to occur during the 9 months involved in any following pregnancy. The other category contained 634 pregnancies that were seizure-free in the pre-pregnancy year. Multivariate logistic regressions were fitted for 1345 subjects for seizure occurrence during pregnancy for co-variates listed to identify statistically seizure-related co-variates. RESULTS: Seizure-affected pregnancy rates in the 1345 pregnancies studied remained stable over the consecutive 5-year intervals from 1999 onwards, viz. 50.5%, 53.9%, 50.3%, 52.8% and 48.2%. Seizure-affected pregnancy was more likely in WWE, who had: (1) Shorter durations of pre-pregnancy freedom from seizures. (2) Focal epilepsy. (3) Not taken ASMs while pregnant. (4) Not used pre-pregnancy folate supplementation. (5) Longer durations of epilepsy. (6) Earlier age onset of epilepsy. The hazard of a seizure-affected next pregnancy was over threefold higher when the pre-pregnancy year was seizure-affected than when it had been seizure-free. SIGNIFICANCE: The finding that a year's pre-pregnancy seizure freedom was associated with a reduced risk of seizures in the next pregnancy was expected. The magnitude of the greater than threefold higher seizure risk in the next pregnancy if seizures had occurred in the pre-pregnancy year was not. It identifies a group of women with epilepsy whose medical management during pregnancy warrants particular care.

摘要：INTRODUCTION: To evaluate adjunctive cenobamate efficacy by focal seizure subtype during the open-label extension (OLE) of a phase 3 randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled study (C035) in Asian patients with focal seizures. METHODS: Patients aged 18-70 years old with uncontrolled focal seizures despite treatment with 1-3 antiseizure medications who completed the double-blind study (n = 425) could enter the 52-week OLE, comprising a 20-week blinded conversion from placebo or 100, 200, or 400 mg/day cenobamate to a target cenobamate dose of 400 mg/day before entering a 32-week open-label maintenance phase at 300 mg/day. 摘要：Sarcococca saligna (S. saligna), a medicinal plant, has traditionally been used due to its beneficial effects on the nervous system. This study evaluated the anticonvulsant efficacy of S. saligna methanolic extract (SSME) against pentylenetetrazol induced seizures (60 mg/kg) in Wistar rats. Sodium valproate (300 mg/kg, administered orally) served as the reference drug. Rats received SSME at doses of 250, 500, and 1000 mg/kg for seven days. Standard anterior temporal lobectomy using a retractorless technique in patients with drug-resistant mesial temporal lobe epilepsy: long-term seizure outcomes and predictors of postoperative seizure freedom.推荐
PMID 42975722 · Neurosurgical review · 2026 11 03
作者：Chaisawasthomrong C, Sitthinamsuwan B, Tansirisithikul C, Jetjumnong C et al.
链接：PubMed · DOI
命中检索：不限期刊主题候选（待筛选）
发表形式：期刊论文（研究设计待核对）
摘要：BACKGROUND: Fixed brain retractors risk focal ischemia and cortical injury. Although retractorless dynamic retraction is established in skull base surgery, its role in epilepsy surgery remains poorly characterized. OBJECTIVE: To evaluate long-term seizure outcomes, safety, and reliability of retractorless standard anterior temporal lobectomy (ATL) in drug-resistant mesial temporal lobe epilepsy (MTLE), and identify predictors of seizure freedom. METHODS: We retrospectively analyzed 42 patients who underwent retractorless standard ATL for drug-resistant MTLE. Dynamic retraction used bipolar forceps and pressure-controlled suction. The primary outcome was seizure freedom (Engel Class Ia) at minimum 24-month follow-up; secondary outcomes were complications and resection adequacy on postoperative magnetic resonance imaging (MRI). Predictors were identified using multivariable logistic regression and Cox proportional hazards models. RESULTS: At a mean follow-up of 66.3 months, 69% of patients achieved complete seizure freedom (Engel Class Ia), and 83.3% achieved freedom from disabling seizures (Engel Class I). Postoperative MRI confirmed adequate resection in 95.2% of cases. Complications were uncommon and unrelated to retraction injury. Low preoperative seizure frequency (< 3 seizures/week) was the sole independent predictor of seizure freedom (odds ratio, 24.21; P = 0.026), while higher frequency (≥ 3 seizures/week) was associated with increased recurrence risk (hazard ratio, 7.34; P = 0.042). CONCLUSION: Retractorless standard ATL is safe and effective for drug-resistant MTLE, achieving long-term seizure control while minimizing retraction-associated risks.
